# Internal capability audit digest (repository truth, September 2026)

> **Generated evidence appendix (2026-09-05/06).** Output of a seven-dimension audit of what the repository actually ships, run for `Docs/MarketAnalysis/MARKET_GAP_PLAN_2026.md`. Each dimension was audited by one agent and then adversarially re-checked by a second (the VERIFIER CORRECTIONS section at the end); where the two disagree the plan followed the code. One verifier claim was itself wrong and is corrected in the plan: the arcade grid is NOT locked on a fresh profile, because `GameModeProgressionService` is instantiated by no shipped scene and every consumer null-checks it open. Status legend: live / partial / dead (code exists, unreachable) / absent / planned. File paths and line numbers are as of commit `e800630d` on `bleeding-edge`.

Status legend: live = reachable by a player in the shipped build; partial = exists but incomplete/gated; dead = code/assets exist but unreachable; absent = does not exist; planned = documented intent only.



## DIMENSION: Multiplayer, matchmaking and social

Cosmic Shore at Steam EA is a friends-only online game with a solid but narrow social spine. Every player who reaches Menu_Main silently hosts their own UGS Relay session (the locked EAGER design) and joins one shared 100-player presence lobby; the in-game Online panel lists every signed-in player, and a click on any row sends a party invite. Accept moves you into the inviter's Relay session, you roam the lava-lamp menu together, the host opens an arcade card, every guest is pulled into a shared lobby, each picks vessel and domain, readies up, and the host launches. Kick (host-only), cancel-invite, disconnect toasts, an AI backfill that fills any card to its seat count, and a bounce-to-solo when the host drops are all live; the 2-player path was live-verified on real machines on 2026-09-02 after a root-cause fix (B16). That is the whole of "play with friends", and it works at party size 4 (6 transport seats, 4 shown).

"Play with strangers" does not exist. The public session query/create/join path in MultiplayerSetup is unreachable in the shipped flow: the eager party session is never null, the early return at MultiplayerSetup.cs:308 always fires, party sessions are created IsPrivate=true, and the prefab carrying that code sits only in four legacy scenes that are not on the shipped 13-card roster. There is no lobby browser, no join code, no Steam friends or overlay invites (Steamworks SDK absent; SignInWithSteamAsync is a stub), no voice or text chat, no spectator, no tournaments; the Port leaderboards screen is PlayFab-backed and dead, leaving the weekly-challenge UGS board as the only ranking. Identity is an anonymous, device-cached UGS account with no linking.

The open risks are reliability, not features: 3-4-player parties are unproven (B5/B4/B6 open; the 4-VP exit criteria have never been recorded green), host-loss ends the party for everyone (no migration, no rejoin), the ecology is simulated per-peer in every mode but one so two players do not see the same world, movement is client-authoritative with unconditional connection approval, and offline mode is code-complete but not editor-verified with its Reconnect button not placed in any scene or prefab. For a hardcore player there is no ladder, no ranked play, no integrity guarantees; for a casual player the friction is finding a friend by a Pilot#### name in a list of strangers, with no Steam glue.

Invites are only receivable in Menu_Main; players IN A MATCH are shown non-invitable (FriendsListPanel.ResolveRemoteStatus:378). ### Strengths
- A complete, documented, live-verified friends path on real machines (invite → join → shared lobby → launch → return → re-invite) after the 2026-09-02 root-cause fix, with kick, cancel-invite, disconnect notices and host-loss recovery all in place (Docs/PartySystem/BUGS.md B10/B12/B13/B16).
- Every shipped card is playable solo or with any party size 1-4 because AI backfill fills the remaining seats up to the card's max (12 on Joust/Skim Race); the same pipeline runs offline on a loopback host, so 'no internet' still means 'a full match'.
- One unified server-authoritative spawn/scene pipeline (menu, AI, gameplay, party guest) and a locked eager-Relay design with a state machine and named recovery for every failure class — the architecture is sound and heavily documented (Docs/PartySystem/ARCHITECTURE.md, MultiplayerArchitecture dossier).
- Party-in-the-menu is a genuine social space: guests roam the lava lamp together, use toys, and are pulled into whatever card the host opens, with late joiners receiving lobby state as replicated data rather than a droppable RPC.
- Presence is global and immediate — every online player is visible and invitable with one click, no codes — which is the right shape for a party game once a friend graph is layered on top.
- No Steam networking dependency: the whole stack works through UGS, so it will function on Steam without Steamworks and could extend to other storefronts or platforms without a transport rewrite.

### Gaps
- [critical] No way to play with strangers: the public session query/join path is unreachable and every party session is private: A $15 solo buyer with no friends who own the game can only ever fight AI; the 'party game for pilots' has no public party. MultiplayerSetup.ExecuteMultiplayerSetup returns at line 308 whenever ActiveSession is set, which the eager party design guarantees, and PartySessionService creates sessions IsPrivate=true; the prefab that carries the path lives only in four un-shipped legacy scenes.
- [critical] 3-4 player party reliability is unproven; the 4-VP exit criteria have never been recorded green and B5/B4/B6/B2 are open: Most modes are designed as domain team races and the launch panel seats four; if the third join fails intermittently the headline party experience breaks in front of the friends you just convinced to buy it.
- [major] No Steam identity, Steam friends, overlay invites or Rich Presence; identity is an anonymous device-cached UGS account with all provider links stubbed: On Steam the expected 'invite a friend' is the overlay; here a friend must be found by Pilot#### name in a list of everyone online. Reinstalls or a second PC create a new anonymous account with no unlocks or friends.
- [major] No host migration and no rejoin: a host quit or crash ends the party for everyone, a dropped guest cannot return to the match: Every match result is hostage to one machine; a 30-minute Maelstrom evaporates on a host disconnect. Documented as an accepted EA absence, but it is the number-one refund-review generator for host-based party games.
- [major] No in-game communication of any kind (voice, text, emotes, pings): Team modes (Astro League, Brood Rush, Dog Fight, Salvo wingman reload) assume coordination that the game gives no channel for; casual groups without Discord play in silence.
- [major] Friends system has no add-friend UI and no rendered friends list; block has no UI: The persistent social graph cannot form in-game, so every 'that was fun' ends with the other player vanishing from the presence list; there is also no way to keep a griefer out after a kick.
- [major] World consistency and integrity: ecology is simulated per-peer in every mode but one, movement is client-authoritative, connection approval is unconditional, no ping shown in release: Two players in the same Rampage or Wildlife Liberation match are shooting different creatures; kills are self-reported over ServerRpc. Harmless among friends, disqualifying for any future stranger matchmaking or ranking.
- [major] Offline mode is not editor-verified and its Reconnect button is placed in no scene or prefab: The investor checkpoint commits to 'launching with no network reaches the menu with a clear message'; the code exists but the human gate has not run and the way back online is missing from the UI.
- [minor] Per-mode leaderboards are dead (PlayFab-backed Port screen, LeaderboardManager prefab in no scene); only the weekly-challenge board is live and depends on three unenforced UGS dashboard settings: The only competitive surface is one weekly time-trial; a wrongly-configured dashboard ships an upside-down board with no error.
- [minor] Presence and invites are poll-based (1.5 s / 0.75 s boosted) over one 100-player lobby with no scale or cost plan: Fine for a launch of dozens; a Steam-front-page spike fragments discovery and burns Relay allocations from every idle menu.
- [minor] Join failures and mid-match disconnects are not instrumented; NetDiag is stripped from release: Launch-week party reliability will be anecdotal; the team cannot see B5-class failures happening in the wild.

### Quick wins
- Ship a 'Quick Match' / 'Open to public' toggle by reviving the existing MultiplayerSetup query/create/join path: create the party session with IsPrivate=false when the host opts in, tag gameMode/maxPlayers session properties (already written in GetSessionProperties), and add a 'Join a public party' button that runs QuerySessions + TryJoinFirstAvailable — the code is written, it only needs the early-return at MultiplayerSetup.cs:308 routed around and the ActiveSession leave/join sequence reused from PartyInviteController.
- Run the 3-VP and 4-VP MPPM gates (TESTS.md S-series, Stress-2) with uniquely-tagged clones on the post-B16 build and record the result in MPPM_SESSION_LOG.md; if B5 reproduces, that single finding outranks every feature below.
- Place ReconnectButton and run FrogletTools > Interface > Wire Offline Menu Surfaces on Menu_Main, then execute the offline checklist in Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md — the investor checkpoint promises this behaviour.
- Add an 'Add Friend by name' field and a Friends section to FriendsListPanel (FriendsServiceFacade.SendFriendRequestByNameAsync and FriendsDataSO.Friends already exist), plus a Block ✕ on OnlineInfoEntry wired to BlockPlayerAsync — a day of UI over a finished backend.
- Instrument party_join_failed (reason from NetworkDiagnostics.ClassifyException) and network_disconnected in AnalyticsServiceFacade so launch-week reliability is measurable; both are already specced in Docs/Analytics/event-taxonomy.md.
- Surface RTT to host in the release HUD (UnityTransport.GetCurrentRtt is already read in DiagnosticsHUD.cs:204-213 behind a DEVELOPMENT_BUILD guard) so players can tell lag from a bug.
- Flip the Wildlife Liberation sharks' NetworkSynced opt-in through the MPPM verification owed by Docs/ECOSYSTEM_NETWORK_SYNC.md, then extend it to the other shipped fauna configs — the replication layer is code-complete and B16's guard makes the un-synced prefabs safe.
- Remove MultiplayerSetup.prefab from the four un-shipped legacy scenes (or delete those scenes) so the dead matchmaking path stops being mistaken for a feature in docs and reviews.
- Add a 'Party disbanded — host left' 1-button popup (INVITE_ENHANCEMENTS Task 0 machinery) and show the remaining members each other's names so they can re-invite in one tap after a bounce.

### Open questions
- Has any 3- or 4-human party (not MPPM clones) completed invite → match → return → re-invite on the post-B16 build? The live verification on 2026-09-02 was two machines.
- What UGS plan and Relay/Lobby quota is the EA launch budgeted against, given every idle player holds a Relay allocation and discovery is one 100-player lobby? Is there a CCU number the team is planning for?
- Is Steam-linked authentication (SignInWithSteamAsync is a stub) planned before or after launch? Without it an anonymous account is lost on reinstall and Steam friends stay invisible.
- Does the Relay region selection (WithRelayNetwork() with no region argument) pick by QoS, and has a transatlantic party been tested? The B11 report was misread as distance once already.
- Will the Wildlife Liberation sharks ship with NetworkSynced on, or is the release build fully per-peer ecology? The docs say code-complete, B16 says every shipped config is off, and 4 assets now say on.
- Is the weekly-challenge UGS leaderboard configured (ascending, keep-best, weekly reset with archive) in the production dashboard? The code can only detect the sort-order failure at runtime.
- Is host migration or rejoin-in-progress on the post-EA roadmap with a date, or is 'host quit ends the party' the intended experience through 1.0?
- What is the intended answer for a solo buyer with no friends in-game — is AI-only the launch story, or is a public quick-match expected before Steam reviews land?


## DIMENSION: Onboarding and the first 15 minutes

A fresh Windows install boots straight through: Bootstrap auto-signs in anonymously (AppManager.cs:152/610), the Authentication scene auto-skips when signed in and only stops a player to type a name if their profile is a generated "Pilot####" (AuthenticationSceneController.cs:354-362), with a 10 s safety timeout and a working offline loopback-host fallback. The player lands on Home with a Settings button, an Arcade button and an unlabelled freestyle toggle; the Arcade modal shows all 13 modern modes (OrganicRematchGames wired on AppManager.prefab:61) with a live two-phase preview window, a single launch panel (intensity, briefing, derived ability/control rows, roster, Start), and AI backfill that seats empty chairs at launch. A solo hardcore player can reach a real match in Skim Race, Joust, Scurry or Maelstrom in roughly 2-4 minutes of clicks plus a heavy arena load, a 6 s (skippable) cinematic, an in-scene Ready press and a 3-2-1 countdown. That is the whole onboarding. Nothing teaches the core loop: the FTUE under Assets/FTUE is dead code (its trigger `FTUEEventManager.OnInitializeFTUECalled` is never called and TutorialFlowController is in no scene or prefab), the quest chain that CLAUDE.md, MENU_PROGRESSION_AND_IAP.md and the investor checkpoint call "the guided progression" is not instantiated on this branch (GameModeProgressionService exists only in `MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/PlayerDataService.prefab`, 0 references in Bootstrap/Menu_Main/Authentication), so every card and intensity is unlocked and the Profile-screen quest track is inert; dialogue content is sample text and an empty `ftue_intro_01`; the Codex is data with no runtime UI; only Joust has an idle hint toast; the stick primer row was retired and no mode authors a replacement (ModeControlsLibrary DefaultRows: []). Controls are keyboard dual-WASD by default (the starter Squirrel is a two-stick hull), single-stick mouse only on one-thumb hulls (reachable via the accidentally-unlocked Scarab), gamepad detected by actuation, no rebinding, no sensitivity, and only four keyboard glyph labels exist so the Q/E chips draw blank. Briefing copy is thin ("Joust joust joust", "Classic Joust"). The first-session validation task C2 has not run; 59 of 64 QA backlog items have never been executed. A hardcore player who already knows arcade flight games can be in a fun match unaided in about five minutes; a casual player will fly a keyboard two-stick scheme with no steering primer and no explanation of prisms, domains, cells, elementals or the nucleus, and will get there only by trial.

### Strengths
- Time to a real match for someone who already knows arcade flight: auto sign-in, one Arcade click, one card, Start — solo AI backfill and offline loopback host mean no lobby wait and no internet dependency (ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:1222-1229, OfflineModeService.cs).
- The launch panel is a live legend: the hull's four abilities and their exact physical control are derived from the ability map and glyph set, and the preview window lets you fly the arena before committing (Docs/ArcadeLaunch/ARCHITECTURE.md §4, 17 ModePreview definitions).
- The in-match objective readout states the metric and target for every mode off ScoringMetric (ObjectiveIconSet.asset, GoalStack in both GameCanvas prefabs).
- The connecting panel is honest: a monotonic bar, a live arena preview and a pilot roster, capped at 45 s, so a long load never reads as a hang (ConnectingPanelController.cs).
- Freestyle is the player's own vessel already flying behind the menu; handing over control is one press, and there is no clock, score or failure state in which to learn a hull.
- Maelstrom's intensity-1 pool (Joust, Skim Race, Scurry) is Squirrel-eligible, so the always-unlocked meta-mode is coherent with the starter vessel (MaelstromData.asset IntensityTiers).

### Gaps
- [critical] There is no teaching layer of any kind in the build: FTUE never starts, quest chain service is not instantiated, dialogue has no lines, Codex has no UI, only one idle hint exists, and the stick primer is retired with no replacement.: Every document (README, CLAUDE.md, MENU_PROGRESSION_AND_IAP.md, the investor checkpoint) describes a guided progression that the code on this branch does not run. A new player is handed 12 unranked cards, a two-stick keyboard scheme and a vocabulary (domain, mass, cell, elemental, nucleus) that nothing explains.
- [critical] The starter vessel forces the least casual desktop control scheme (dual-WASD twin-stick emulation) with no rebinding, no sensitivity, and blank Q/E chips; the mouse scheme exists only for hulls the player does not own or reaches by accident (Scarab).: The first minute of flight decides retention; the scheme is unexplained and un-tunable, and Invert Y does nothing on the very hulls the mouse scheme serves.
- [critical] Docs and the investor checkpoint promise a quest chain and a controls overlay as the onboarding, and C2 validation, quest dialogue (C9) and quest data (C10/C11) are all still open; the referenced quest-graph implementation lives on an unmerged branch.: The plan's onboarding is content and wiring that does not exist on bleeding-edge; the Steam EA date is being estimated against it.
- [major] Time-to-fun is padded by a heavy arena load, a 6 s cinematic, a second in-scene Ready press and a countdown on every match and every Maelstrom round.: For a party game of 2-5 minute rounds the ceremony can exceed the round; the repo has no measured cold-load table to size it.
- [major] Briefing and objective copy is placeholder on most cards ('Joust joust joust', 'Classic Joust', 'Skim the tracks.') and no card has Tips.: The launch panel rotates description and tips as the only in-menu explanation of a mode; with tips empty and descriptions terse it explains nothing.
- [major] Onboarding analytics cannot locate a stall: only game_first_launched and game_completed exist; no first-match or time-to-first-match event.: Without it the team cannot 'fix only what actually stops them' as C2 intends.
- [minor] Vessel-locked modes launch with a hull the player does not own and the launch panel shows an empty ship slot (class silently falls back to Dolphin in the modal state).: Works by accident of the spawn clamp; the panel reads as broken and the 4000-crystal hangar economy is contradicted by every mode handing out the hull for free.
- [minor] Scarab is unlocked by asset state (isLocked 0) without ownedFromStart; docs list Squirrel as the only free vessel.: Either an economy leak or an unrecorded design decision; it is also the only way a new player meets the mouse scheme.

### Quick wins
- Author FlightControl rows in Resources/ModeControlsLibrary.asset DefaultRows (steer, throttle/E, boost/Shift, Escape = overview) — one asset edit, appears on every card and matches the existing derived-row system.
- Add keyboardLabel entries for Q, E, WASD and P;L' to Resources/ControlGlyphSet.asset so no HUD chip draws blank on keyboard.
- Rewrite Description and add 2-3 Tips per roster card and real ObjectiveText on the 17 ModePreview definitions (Joust/SkimRace/Scurry first, since they are the intensity-1 Maelstrom pool).
- Decide the quest gate: either drop GameModeProgressionService into Bootstrap.unity (it is already authored and every consumer null-checks) or remove the dead QuestTrackView from ProfileScreen so the Profile page does not show an inert quest track.
- Auto-ready when there is exactly one human in the session and default the pre-game cinematic to skippable-by-any-input, removing the second Ready press and up to 6 s per round.
- Author idle-hint toasts for the 12 modes that have none (GameToastConfig_* assets; only Joust has one) — the system already fires them after 60 s of inactivity.
- Add a 'game_started' / first-match analytics event next to game_first_launched so C3's median time-to-Maelstrom is answerable.
- Run the C2 first-session observation on the current build now, before quest content lands — the 'first 15 minutes' being shipped is the one without it.
- Put a static controls card (keyboard + pad) behind the pause menu's existing ControllerHintImage slot.

### Open questions
- Is the quest chain meant to be live at EA launch? GameModeProgressionService is authored and consumed everywhere but instantiated nowhere on bleeding-edge; the quest-graph FTUE lives on the unmerged `claude/ftue-editor-tool-69acq5` branch.
- Which hull should a brand-new keyboard player fly first? Squirrel (two-stick) is the only owned starter; the mouse scheme only exists for one-thumb hulls.
- Is Scarab intended to be free (SO_Class_Scarab.asset isLocked 0, no ownedFromStart), and should vessel-locked modes hand out unowned hulls for free as they do today?
- What are measured cold-load times per mode on the Steam min-spec, and is the 6 s cinematic + in-scene Ready meant to survive for solo/Maelstrom rounds?
- Will input rebinding and mouse sensitivity ship for EA, or is the plan to rely on Steam Input (no Steamworks SDK today)?
- Where will the core-loop vocabulary (domain, mass, cell, elementals, nucleus) be taught — quest dialogue, Codex UI, in-match hints — and which of those is funded in Workstream C?


## DIMENSION: Mastery, skill ceiling and competitive integrity

Skill expression today lives almost entirely inside the vessels, and four of them are genuinely deep: the Squirrel's analog drift on a vector flight model (grip-before-thrust, 1.25x drift overshoot, recover by aiming out of the slide), the Rhino's analog two-trigger sword with true point-velocity impacts and a hold-to-energize ritual, the Sparrow's four-stage spray-accuracy curve (2 s perfect, 1.5 deg plateau, 7.5 deg blow-out) plus indefinite afterburner and turret stance, and the Dolphin's skim-to-bank / crystal-to-fire economy with a drift-bought squared boost. The Scarab adds a free juke that is also a swept-plate blast, the sanctioned steal, and a switch/dais economy. Four vessels (Sparrow, Dolphin, Squirrel, Urchin) have complete elemental maps with all four level-5 upgrades; Scarab is 3/4; Manta, Rhino and Serpent still have open design slots, so their element flowers change little. A Rocket League or Trackmania player would find real mechanical depth in a drift-and-joust Skim Race and in the Rhino/Sparrow duels.

What they would find unfair or shallow is the competitive scaffolding. The ElementalComebackSystem is a REQUIRED component in every party game with no player or lobby toggle; the per-mode rate defaults to 1.0 level per unit of deficit, so in Joust (race to 3) being ONE joust behind lifts every element of the trailing team to level 10 and unlocks all four level-5 upgrades, and in Skim Race a 10-crystal deficit does the same. Movement is client-authoritative (ClientNetworkTransform) and hits are owner-reported with only enum validation, so there is no anti-cheat; the host is a player. Fauna simulation is server-authoritative for only 4 of the lifeform configs, so Wildlife Liberation's targets still diverge per peer. AI difficulty is intensity (skill = intensity x 0.25) lerping throttle/aggression, with abilities fired on fixed timers; the 13 "AI profiles" are just names and avatars.

Competitive surfaces are thin: no ranked/ELO/matchmaking (friends-party only), no ghosts or time trials, no spectator, no replay beyond Astro League's goal ghost, one leaderboard (the weekly challenge's completion time, fastest first, one attempt a day, Friends tab shipped off, no in-run readout, no reward), and the legacy PORT leaderboards screen is disabled in Menu_Main and reads a dead PlayFab statistic. Per-mode best scores, wins and vessel telemetry are written to Cloud Save but no UI reads them. Luck is moderate: crystal respawns are random in the nucleus, Maelstrom draws mode AND intensity at random each round, and the arena build is deterministic per seed. Most modes were still "never opened" by QA as of 2026-08-13; only QA-BUILD-COMPILE has a recorded pass.

### Capabilities
- [LIVE] Squirrel drift + vector flight model + tube/boost ring: The deepest flight model in the fleet: analog drift depth, overshoot payoff bounded so it never brakes, joust = be faster at the pass. Only Skim Race and Joust list the Squirrel (ArcadeGameJoust/SkimRace Vessels = SO_Class_Squirrel). No edit-mode test guards the scalar/vector identity (SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md §9).
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md:13-70 (two drift tiers 1.4/0.5 and 1.8/0.25 on one analog trigger; vector model thrust along nose; grip before thrust); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md:180-200 (fleet status: Squirrel/Scarab/Dolphin vector, everyone else scalar; driftOvershootCeiling 1.25, RotationThrottleScaler 0); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SQUIRREL_TUBE.md:1-40 (RT places a danger-prism tube 1 s of travel ahead); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/ImpactEffects/EffectsSO/Vessel Skimmer Effects/VesselExplosionBySkimmerEffectSO.cs:69-76 (joust point only when faster and opposing domain); Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Squirrel.asset (Live Wire / Heavy Trail / Shepherd / Twin Rings)
  - hardcore fit: Real racing-line + drift-recovery mastery; overshoot ceiling rewards clean long drifts; joust is a speed-at-contact duel. Keyboard gets binary drift (Shift = 1/0), so full depth is gamepad-only. | casual fit: One trigger drifts, one trigger places a tube; starter vessel so it is the first thing a new player flies.
- [PARTIAL] Rhino analog sword (swing kinematics, energize ritual): Mechanically the richest melee model in the game and the basis of Astro League's sword-strike ball physics. Partial because: analog stepping bug open, remote peers see an event-driven approximation of the swing (RHINO_SHIELD_SWIPE.md:45-68), keyboard has no analog pose, and the Rhino's element map is 1/4 designed so its four ability cards are mostly LOCKED. QA-VESSEL-RHINO-SWORD and QA-RHINO-RAMP-BOOST unrun (Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:984,1165).
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/RHINO_SHIELD_SWIPE.md:18-44 (difference axis = lateral swipe up to 90 deg, sum axis = chop to 65 deg); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/RHINO_SHIELD_SWIPE.md:137-170 (SkimmerSwingKinematics: v(P) = v_vessel + omega x r + R*v_rel; tip hits harder than hilt); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/RHINO_ENERGY_SWORD.md:36-60 (hold both triggers 1 s to ENERGIZE; only an energized blade pops super-shielded prisms); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/RHINO_SHIELD_SWIPE.md:363-380 (Known issue OPEN: analog stepping on the designer's setup); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/IO/KeyboardInputStrategy.cs:131-139 (Shift keys write trigger analog as 1f/0f); Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Rhino.asset:19-52 (three of four element mappings 'not yet designed')
  - hardcore fit: Tip-velocity strikes and the energize window are a real timing skill; Astro League swings reward swing speed over hull speed. | casual fit: Pull a trigger, the sword swings; the ritual is discoverable but unexplained in-game.
- [LIVE] Sparrow spray accuracy, afterburner, turret stance, skyburst: Complete map (4/4 upgrades). Trigger discipline (pulse for accuracy) is a learnable skill with a haptic ramp on pad. Several Sparrow QA items unrun (QA-SPARROW-BOOST-WARD, -TURRET-STANCE partial, -MISSILE-BAY).
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SPARROW_SPRAY_ACCURACY.md:1-17 (2 s perfect, cone opens to 1.5 deg, holds 2 s, blows out to 7.5 deg at 10 s; release resets); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SPARROW_SPRAY_ACCURACY.md:565-626 (four-part curve table; 180 rounds/s; Mass grows rounds 3x-6x); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SPARROW_AFTERBURNER.md:1-22 (indefinite boost, base strafing roll, Time-5 Elemental Ward); Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Sparrow.asset (Piercing Bullets / Elemental Ward / Shielded Prisms / Domain-Safe Skybursts); Assets/_SO_Assets/Scoring Rules/DogFightScoringRule.asset:16-18 (bulletPoints 1, missilePoints 50)

- [PARTIAL] Dolphin skim economy, drift-bought boost, Echo Sight: A tight bank/spend economy with a public aim telegraph (Echo Sight replicates to every peer). Partial because the skim path was only recently rewired and has no recorded QA pass; Rampage and The Bends depend on it entirely.
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/DOLPHIN_ENERGY_ECONOMY.md:11-80 (Energy banked +0.0067 per skim, halved on ram, spent to 0 on crystal; no passive regen); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/DOLPHIN_ENERGY_ECONOMY.md:153-190 (boost fills while drifting 3.636 s, peak multiplier squared: 68 -> 347 u/s); Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Dolphin.asset (Pilot Echo / Claimed Seed / Clean Blast / Drift Ward); Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:312-345 (QA-DOLPHIN-SKIM: 'nobody has ever seen a Dolphin skim work'); CLAUDE.md § 'A skimmer only skims if VesselStatus points AT it' (Dolphin shipped with the wrong skimmer referenced for its whole life; Serpent still fails the audit)

- [PARTIAL] Scarab juke / steal / cavitation plate / ball forge: The designated Rocket League analogue. Space L5 label is empty and Mass L5 is dead by the doc's own admission; the SCARAB.md is still framed as a design proposal with 15 open questions (§15).

### Strengths
- Genuine per-vessel mechanical depth on four hulls: analog drift on a vector flight model with a bounded overshoot payoff (SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md), a rigid-body sword whose tip hits harder than its hilt (RHINO_SHIELD_SWIPE.md §Swing velocity model), a four-stage spray-accuracy curve that rewards trigger discipline (SPARROW_SPRAY_ACCURACY.md Round 6), and a skim/bank/fire economy with a public aim telegraph (DOLPHIN_ENERGY_ECONOMY.md).
- Element levels are per-run only and no IAP exists, so persistent progression never buys in-match power (ECONOMY_AND_PRICING.md:17; VesselStatus.cs:280).
- Deterministic, shared arenas: Skim Race tracks are seeded and replicated, environments and Dog Fight crystals build identically from serialized seeds, and crystal respawn RNG is bounded to the nucleus (CrystalManager.cs:447-465).
- Scoring, turn flow, winner and placement are server-authoritative and domain-aggregated, with golf-rule sentinels centralised so scoreboards cannot drift (GolfScoreSentinels.cs; ScoringRuleSO family).
- A real global time-attack surface exists in the weekly challenge (completion time, ascending, only finishers submit) and Astro League has a goal replay ghost.
- Maelstrom gives a multi-round placement series (2/1/0, race to 6) with intensity-tiered pools, the closest thing to a tournament format.
- AI navigation is measured (400/400 objectives after the Dubins break-off) and every mode backfills to a full lobby, so solo play is always available.

### Gaps
- [critical] Mandatory, un-toggleable comeback rubber-band whose default rate (1.0) is catastrophic in the race modes: one joust or ten crystals behind puts the trailing team at level 10 in every element and switches on all four level-5 upgrades.: A hardcore player reads this as the game punishing a lead; the doc trail (DogFight/Bends/Wildlife) already records the rate must be re-derived per target, but SkimRace, Joust, Scurry, AstroLeague and BroodRush never authored one and inherit 1.0.
- [critical] No competitive scaffolding: no ranked/ELO, no matchmaking queue, no spectator, no match replay, no ghosts or time trials, no visible personal bests or win/loss record.: 'A casual game for hardcore gamers' has nothing for a hardcore player to climb, review or benchmark against beyond one weekly time board; the cloud records exist but no UI reads them.
- [major] Client-trusted simulation: owner-authoritative transforms, client-reported hits/kills with enum-only validation, participant host, and ecology synced for only 4 lifeform configs so Wildlife Liberation/Rampage/Salvo objectives resolve against per-peer worlds.: Acceptable for a friends party, unacceptable for any public or ranked play; it also means two players in the same Wildlife Liberation match are literally hunting different creatures.
- [major] Fleet asymmetry in depth: Manta, Rhino and Serpent have no designed element upgrades (Input 0, empty labels), Urchin has no HUD, Scarab's Mass-5 is dead and Space-5 unlabelled.: Six of ten vessels either lack the ability-upgrade layer or display it as LOCKED, so the 'collect crystals to power up' loop is real only on part of the roster.
- [major] AI difficulty is intensity x 0.25 lerping throttle/aggression; abilities fire on fixed timers, AIGunner is a stub, profiles are names+avatars, and some prefabs author Low == High so skill does nothing.: Bots cannot serve as a mastery ladder; a solo hardcore player has no way to get a hard opponent on an easy track.
- [major] Device asymmetry: keyboard triggers are binary so analog drift/sword depth is pad-only, Invert Y is dead on one-thumb vessels, no rebinding UI, PlayStation glyphs show Xbox letters.: A Windows/Steam launch will be keyboard-heavy; the mechanics that define the skill ceiling are unreachable there.
- [minor] Weekly challenge is half-wired: no in-run progress readout, card fields/button partially unwired, no reward, Friends scope off, region from device country.: The one leaderboard the game has is hard to engage with and pays nothing.
- [minor] Every mode is one round/one turn with no series, overtime (except AL golden goal) or tiebreak beyond enum order; losers all tie on a sentinel score.: Reduces competitive texture; a 2-4 player race has no comeback beyond the buff and no 'best of three'.
- [major] Most skill-bearing content has no recorded QA pass; three modes were 'never opened' and the Dolphin skim 'never seen working' as of the last backlog scan.: Balance and feel claims in this audit rest on measurement scripts and code, not play; the investor doc's 'feature-complete' is a code claim.

### Quick wins
- Author ComebackRatePerScoreDeficit explicitly on SkimRace/Joust/Scurry/AstroLeague/BroodRush cards using the 'quarter-of-target deficit buys ~1 level' rule the other modes already follow (e.g. Joust ~1.3, Scurry ~0.2, SkimRace ~0.1), and add a build check like author_bends_assets.py so a default can never ship again. One asset edit per mode.
- Expose a comeback toggle in the launch panel (a bool on ArcadeGameConfigSO that zeroes gameData.ComebackRatePerScoreDeficit when off) so a party can opt into 'competitive' rules; the system already treats rate <= 0 as opt-out (ElementalComebackSystem.cs:305).
- Surface the stats you already store: a Hangar/Profile row reading ModeStatsCloudData (best time per mode/intensity, games won) and HangarCloudData vessel records. Data and writers exist (UGSStatsManager.cs:128-231); only a reader is missing.
- Show the player's stored personal best next to the finish time on the Skim Race/Joust scoreboard via UGSStatsManager.GetEvaluatedHighScore, giving a time-attack loop with no new persistence.
- Bind WeeklyChallengeService.OnAttemptProgress to a HUD readout (achieved/target/seconds) and wire the card's ObjectiveText/StatusText — the doc lists both as the natural next step.
- Add a bot-difficulty stepper to the launch panel that feeds ConfigureForGameMode's skill argument independently of intensity (one float, ServerPlayerVesselInitializerWithAI.cs:464), and fix prefabs where Low == High so the dial does something.
- Give keyboard players analog-ish triggers: ramp Shift over ~0.3 s (like touch drift's DRIFT_EASE_SPEED) so drift depth and sword pose are at least reachable; today they are 1/0.
- Re-derive comeback and end-target values only after a recorded play session per mode; run QA-DOLPHIN-SKIM, QA-VESSEL-RHINO-SWORD and the three never-opened modes before publishing any balance claims.

### Open questions
- Does a vessel-locked mode (Rampage/Bends Dolphin, DogFight/Salvo/Wildlife Sparrow, PeelTheCage/AstroLeague Rhino, ScarabScramble Scarab) refuse or allow a player who has not unlocked that hull? No unlock check was found in ArcadeGameConfigureModal/MinigameLaunchPanel; if allowed, unlocks are purely for freestyle/Skim Race/Joust choice; if refused, a new player is limited to the Squirrel modes for ~20 wins.
- Has the server-authoritative fauna sync (Docs/ECOSYSTEM_NETWORK_SYNC.md) been MPPM-verified, and is the plan to flip NetworkSynced on for all species before EA? Only 4 configs opt in today.
- Are the UGS dashboard settings for the weekly_challenge leaderboard (ascending sort, keep-best, weekly reset with archive) actually configured? Code can only detect a wrong sort order at runtime.
- What is the intended comeback rate for the three default-1.0 race modes — was 1.0 ever playtested in Joust (race to 3) with human opponents?
- Is Steam Input expected to provide controller remapping without the Steamworks SDK, and does the PlayStation-glyph mismatch matter for the launch audience?
- The Rhino analog-stepping bug is attributed to the designer's pad/driver; has it been reproduced on a second controller?
- Is any public matchmaking (quick match against strangers) planned for EA, or is friends-party-plus-bots the whole launch multiplayer story? The presence lobby lists strangers but only invites connect them.


## DIMENSION: Content: modes, vessels, session shape

The repository carries 39 `SO_ArcadeGame` assets, but a player today can reach exactly 13 of them: the 12 cards in `Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/GameLists/OrganicRematchGames.asset` (the only list injected via `AppManager.prefab` `gameList` → `ArcadeExploreView` / `ArcadeLoadoutView`) plus Maelstrom, which is deliberately excluded from the grid and launched from its own control. Twenty assets point at scenes that do not exist on disk (Elimination, Dolphin Darts, Block Bandit, Maze Run, Slip N Stride, …) and six more have scenes in Build Settings but no card that can reach them (single-player Duel for the Cell and Wildlife Blitz, Online Duel, 2v2 Co-op vs AI, Co-op Wildlife Blitz, Multiplayer Freestyle). Every reachable mode is a domain race with AI backfill, a ready-up lobby and a live preview (Salvo excepted), and eleven of the twelve are vessel-locked. Of the twelve, three (Dog Fight, Wildlife Liberation, Peel the Cage) are recorded in `Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md` as "never been opened", and the only QA item that has ever passed is the compile gate.

The fleet reads as four polished hulls, four half-hulls and four shells. Squirrel, Sparrow, Dolphin and Scarab have wired abilities, a HUD variant, a full four-slot elemental map and are flown by the generic `AIPilot`. Urchin has a complete map and abilities but no HUD prefab; Manta, Rhino and Serpent have HUDs and 1–3 bound actions but empty elemental maps. Grizzly, Termite, Falcon and Shrike have prefabs with no `VesselController`, no action bindings and no entry in `Vessel Prefab Container.asset`, so they cannot be spawned. Modes always force their authored hull (`GameDataSO.ClampSelectedVesselToGame`) regardless of hangar ownership, and freestyle offers all eight spawnable hulls unlocked, so the 4,000-crystal vessel economy (≥20 wins per hull at the 200/50/0 payout) gates nothing a player actually plays — it only gates the Hangar.

Two documented systems are dead as wired: the quest-chain mode/intensity progression (`GameModeProgressionService` lives only on the orphaned `MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/PlayerDataService.prefab`, so every mode and intensity is open on first boot, contradicting `MENU_PROGRESSION_AND_IAP.md`, `ECONOMY_TABLES.md` and the Steam checkpoint), and the Codex (33 authored entries, zero runtime readers). Missions, training games, episodes, FTUE and the legacy daily challenge are likewise inert or point at missing scenes. What is genuinely live and repeatable: the 12-mode arcade, a 7-mode Maelstrom race-to-6 (3–7 rounds, roughly 15–35 min), the weekly challenge, and a rich scoreless freestyle hub with seven unlocked toys and seven cells. Typical single matches run 1–5 minutes (Astro League is a fixed 180 s; Joust/Bends are race-to-3), which suits a party cadence but leaves a solo player with only AI-backfilled versions of the same team races. Breadth (39 cards, 12 hull enums, 7 toys, 8 weekly challenges) currently dilutes the polished core of ~9 modes and 4 vessels.

### Capabilities
- [LIVE] Reachable arcade roster (12 grid cards + Maelstrom): Every reachable mode is a multiplayer domain race launched through one ArcadeGameConfigureModal. The 'ArcadeGames' and 'LaunchPartyAllGames' lists (which include single-player cards) are referenced by prefabs but are NOT the injected list, so their extra cards never appear.

- [LIVE] Skim Race (Hex Race): Deterministic seeded track, golf-time scoring, AI backfill to 12. Roughly 2-5 min per race depending on intensity. QA-ARCADE-SKIMRACE-INTENSITY3 still open (new circuit unverified).

- [LIVE] Joust: Race to 3 jousts; matches are short (often under 2 min). In Maelstrom intensity tier 1.

### Strengths
- Twelve reachable arcade modes plus Maelstrom are all fully wired (scene, turn monitor, scoring rule, AI spawner, end-condition asset, launch card), so the visible roster is not vapour — Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/GameLists/OrganicRematchGames.asset and Build Settings agree.
- Four vessels (Squirrel, Sparrow, Dolphin, Scarab) are genuinely complete: bound abilities, HUD variant, full 4x4 elemental map, AI-flyable — and they carry every vessel-locked mode.
- Maelstrom is a real meta-structure (7-mode pool, cumulative intensity tiers, race-to-6, hub between rounds) and is always reachable from first boot.
- Solo/offline play works everywhere through AI backfill on a local host; six cards allow a single player.
- The freestyle hub is unusually rich (7 toys unlocked, 7 authored cells, 16 paintings, 48 Wanderway recipes) and doubles as a friends' sandbox.
- Weekly challenge is live, self-bootstrapping, and gives the game its one real global leaderboard.
- Short, hard-capped rounds (1-5 min; Astro League exactly 180 s) match a party cadence.
- Mode preview window gives 11 of 12 cards a live arena preview before launch.

### Gaps
- [critical] Three of the twelve grid modes (Dog Fight, Wildlife Liberation, Peel the Cage) have never been opened, and the Dolphin skim that Rampage/Bends depend on has never been seen working; only the compile gate has ever passed QA.: A quarter of the paid roster is unverified content shipping to a $15 Steam audience; the QA ledger says so in the team's own words.
- [critical] The documented quest-chain progression (mode and intensity unlocks, hangar gate) is dead: GameModeProgressionService is only on an orphaned migration prefab, so everything is open and nothing guides a new player.: Docs, economy tables and the investor checkpoint all describe a guided ladder that the shipped scene wiring does not run; if wired as authored it would also permanently lock Astro League, Brood Rush and Scarab Scramble and point quest 4 at a hidden mode.
- [major] No reachable single-player content: missions, training, episodes, FTUE and the two single-player scenes are dead or hidden; solo play is only 'multiplayer with bots'.: A 'casual game for hardcore gamers' on Steam needs a solo on-ramp; today the only solo-first surfaces are AI-backfilled team races and a scoreless sandbox.
- [major] Vessel roster is 4 complete / 4 half / 4 shells, while three half-hulls (Manta, Rhino, Serpent) have empty elemental maps yet are offered in Brood Rush, Crystal Capture, Astro League and Peel the Cage.: The elemental upgrade grid — the game's stated buff system — does nothing on the Rhino, which fronts two reachable modes; the HUD honestly shows four LOCKED cards.
- [major] Half the SO_ArcadeGame inventory (20 assets) points at scenes that no longer exist, and six built scenes have no card; docs still count them.: Every doc-quoted mode count is inflated, dead cards sit in Leaderboard/Training/quest lists, and Docs/SCENES.md:282 asserts they share scenes — false in code.
- [minor] Vessel unlock economy gates nothing the player plays (modes force their hull; freestyle offers all eight), so the 20-win-per-hull grind is a hangar-only vanity loop.: The checkpoint calls crystal-earned unlocks 'the progression loop'; in practice a player never needs to buy anything to access all content.
- [minor] Codex is authored (33 entries) but has no runtime UI; Salvo has no preview asset; Scarab is missing from the hangar class list.: Small polish gaps that read as unfinished on a paid Early Access page.
- [major] 3-4 human parties are not dependable and the fauna network sync is unverified, so the ecology-scored modes and the 4-seat Maelstrom the launch plan targets rest on unproven multiplayer paths.: 'Party game for pilots' is the positioning; the team's own roadmap lists 4-player reliability as the top open risk.

### Quick wins
- Decide the progression service's fate in one move: either drop the GameModeProgressionService onto the Bootstrap persistent root (and add AstroLeague/BroodRush/ScarabScramble to alwaysUnlockedModes, replace quest 4's hidden WildlifeBlitz) or delete the quest chain from MENU_PROGRESSION_AND_IAP.md, ECONOMY_TABLES.md Table 5 and the Steam checkpoint so docs match the open-roster build.
- Play Dog Fight, Wildlife Liberation, Peel the Cage and a Dolphin skim once each and log the four QA_BACKLOG items — the cheapest possible de-risk of a quarter of the paid roster.
- Surface the two built single-player scenes (Duel for the Cell, Wildlife Blitz) by adding their cards to OrganicRematchGames.asset, or strip them from Build Settings; either way stop shipping six unreachable scenes.
- Delete or archive the 20 SO_ArcadeGame assets with missing scenes (and the dead LeaderboardGames/Training/Mission lists) so every mode count in docs, codex and UI reflects 12+1.
- Author a ModePreview_Salvo definition (one asset, same pattern as ModePreview_DogFight) so the newest card stops showing 'LEVEL PREVIEW NOT AVAILABLE'.
- Add Scarab to SO_Classlist_Classes.asset so the hangar shows the free beachhead hull; blank Scarab's Space UpgradeLabel is a one-field fix.
- Fill Rhino's ElementalAbilityMap (it fronts Astro League and Peel the Cage) before Manta/Serpent — one asset plus existing wirer tool, per Docs/ElementalAbilitySystem/FLEET_MAPS.md proposals.
- Wire the shipped CodexSO to a minimal read-only panel on the PORT or HANGAR screen; the data, images and resolver already exist.

### Open questions
- Is the quest-chain progression intended for launch (checkpoint says yes) — and if so, who wires GameModeProgressionService into a persistent root, given the orphaned prefab folder is marked DELETE LATER?
- Should Astro League be Rhino-only (CLAUDE.md) or Rhino+Scarab (ArcadeGameAstroLeague.asset lists both)?
- Is the single-player Duel for the Cell / Wildlife Blitz pair meant to return as the solo on-ramp, or are they slated for deletion with the other legacy modes?
- What is the intended role of the vessel unlock economy if every mode force-equips its hull and freestyle ignores locks — hangar cosmetics, or a real gate?
- Which of Manta/Rhino/Serpent/Urchin are in scope for the EA launch fleet, versus being hidden from Brood Rush/Crystal Capture pickers until their elemental maps exist?
- Has any 4-human Maelstrom been completed end to end on the Steam build (checkpoint exit criterion), given ROADMAP.md's open B5 party-reliability item?
- Is Rampage's GolfScoring: 1 on a prisms-destroyed race intentional, or a copy-paste from the time-scored modes?
- Does the weekly challenge's 8-mode pool intentionally include the three modes QA has never opened?


## DIMENSION: Progression, economy and retention hooks

What a player actually gets today is thinner than every economy/progression document claims, and in one place the opposite of what they claim. The only live long-term loop is: win a match → +200 crystals (2nd of three domains +50, last place 0) → buy one of six Hangar vessels at 4,000 crystals (20 wins each, 120 wins for the set). But ownership is economically hollow: 10 of the 12 arcade cards lock to a single hull and `GameDataSO.ClampVesselToGame` forces that hull regardless of whether the player owns it, and the freestyle Vessel Changer toy hands out all 8 hulls with no lock check. A player who never spends a crystal already flies the Dolphin in Rampage/Bends, the Sparrow in Dog Fight/Salvo/Wildlife Liberation, the Rhino in Peel the Cage and the Scarab in Scarab Scramble. Crystals therefore buy only the right to pick a hull in Scurry/Maelstrom/Brood Rush.

The documented guided progression (quest chain → mode unlocks → intensity ladder → Hangar unlock) is not in the build: `GameModeProgressionService` exists only on a stale prefab in `MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/` that no scene references, so `Instance` is null and every consumer null-checks it away — all 12 cards and all four intensities are open from first boot and the Hangar's quest gate is skipped. The quest-claim UI sits on a Profile screen the ScreenSwitcher cannot navigate to (PROFILE falls back to index 5, clamps to the disabled PORT screen). Even if wired, the chain is logically broken (a quest step targets Wildlife Blitz, which has no card; claiming a quest removes the very flag the Hangar gate checks; three live modes are in neither the always-unlocked list nor the chain). The investor checkpoint itself says this layer is "on a separate branch".

What does pull a player back: the weekly challenge (8-mode pool, one run per UTC day, fastest-completion UGS leaderboard with World/Friends tabs) — genuinely live and the only calendar hook. Nothing else: XP was deleted from the profile schema, captains/training/daily-challenge/store/leaderboard-screen/episodes are PlayFab-era dead code, achievements and cosmetics do not exist, per-mode bests are recorded for four modes and displayed nowhere, and the codex has no runtime reader. Identity is anonymous UGS only (every Steam/Google/Apple/Unity sign-in and link method is a `Task.CompletedTask` stub), so Cloud Save roams only with the cached session token: a fresh PC or wiped PlayerPrefs is a new account with zero crystals, no vessels and no weekly bests. After 20 wins a hardcore player has bought a vessel they were already flying; after 120 wins the currency has no sink. A casual player gets the strongest thing the game has — every mode, every intensity and every hull on day one — and a weekly time to beat.

### Capabilities
- [LIVE] Crystal placement payout (soft currency earn): Win = 200, second of three domains = 50, everyone else 0. No participation, loss, streak, first-win, intensity or quest payout. Docs/ECONOMY_AND_PRICING.md §1 still lists the old 5-crystal payout; ECONOMY_TABLES.md and the code agree on 200/50/0.

  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/MaelstromController.cs:22-31 random pool mode + intensity per round, per-domain placement points, race to 6; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameMaelstrom.asset Mode 36, MinPlayers 2 (AI backfill), always excluded from the grid and launched from its own control (ArcadeExploreView.cs:141-147); Each pool game's Scoreboard still pays 200/50/0 with source 'tournament_placement' (Scoreboard.cs:527)
  - hardcore fit: A best-of chain with standings — the closest thing to a ranked set. | casual fit: A party-night format that decides the modes for you.
- [LIVE] Elemental crystal ability levels (in-run progression): The game's real mastery loop resets to zero every match; nothing about elemental play is carried between sessions.
  - evidence: Docs/ECONOMY_TABLES.md Table 1: elemental crystals 'Not persisted'; Assets/_SO_Assets/Classes/SO_Class_Dolphin.asset InitialResourceLevels all 0; ResourceSystem / R_VesselElementalAbilityHandler upgrade at level 5 (CLAUDE.md 'The Four-Icon Ability Row'); comeback buffs via ElementalComebackSystem
  - hardcore fit: Depth exists per match (4 abilities × 4 elements × level-5 upgrades) but no meta-progression records it. | casual fit: Comeback system hands trailing players levels, so a newcomer feels upgrades quickly.
- [PARTIAL] First-launch onboarding / FTUE: A one-time first-launch panel exists; the tutorial framework is unreferenced.
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/UI/Screens/HomeScreen.cs:13-32 FirstAppLaunchScreen shown once via PlayerPrefs key; Assets/FTUE/ TutorialFlowController, FTUEIntroAnimator, InGameTutorialFlowView are referenced by no scene or prefab
  - hardcore fit: n/a | casual fit: No guided first match beyond the mode's controls overlay.

### Strengths
- The weekly challenge is a complete, honest, low-friction return hook: derived offline, one run per UTC day, fastest-time UGS leaderboard with World/Friends tabs, and the card states (BEST / PLAYED TODAY / COMPLETE m:ss.cc) are all live in Menu_Main (WeeklyChallengeService.cs, WeeklyChallengeCard.cs, WeeklyChallengeCatalog.asset).
- The crystal wallet is sound engineering: atomic spend guard, lifetime earned/spent totals, analytics on every earn/spend/blocked spend, immediate cloud flush on entitlement writes (PlayerDataService.cs:511-546).
- Because the progression service is absent, a new player sees every mode, every intensity and every hull on day one — for a party game sold on 'play it your way' this is arguably the right default and it costs nothing to reach fun.
- Maelstrom gives a session a shape (random mode/intensity draws, race-to-6 standings) that a group of friends can treat as a 'set'.
- Offline last-known-good cache means progress and unlocks survive a UGS outage, and the boot chain does not block on the cloud.

### Gaps
- [critical] Guided progression (quest chain → mode unlocks → intensity ladder → Hangar gate) is not in the build: GameModeProgressionService is on an unreferenced 'DELETE LATER' prefab, so Instance is null and every gate silently opens.: Three planning documents and the investor checkpoint describe it as shipped; QA/launch acceptance ('a fresh account completes the quest chain') cannot pass; mode_unlocked/intensity_unlocked analytics never fire.
- [critical] Even when wired, the quest chain cannot complete: WildlifeBlitz(26) has no arcade card; claiming a quest removes the flag the Hangar gate checks; AstroLeague/BroodRush/ScarabScramble are in neither the always-unlocked list nor the chain; the Profile screen hosting the claim UI is unreachable (PROFILE clamps to disabled PORT).: Turning the service back on would lock three live modes forever and make the Hangar unpurchasable through play.
- [critical] Vessel ownership is bypassed by design elsewhere: single-vessel modes force their hull regardless of lock, and the freestyle Vessel Changer offers all eight hulls; the 4,000-crystal price buys almost nothing.: The only crystal sink is hollow, so after ~20 wins there is nothing to chase and after 120 the currency is inert.
- [critical] Identity is anonymous-only; every Steam/social sign-in and link is a stub, so a reinstall on a new machine (or cleared PlayerPrefs) is a new account with zero crystals, no vessels and no weekly bests.: Paid Steam customers expect progress tied to their Steam account; loss is silent and unrecoverable.
- [major] No account-level number that goes up: XP removed from the profile schema, captains/training/daily-challenge/store/leaderboard screens are PlayFab-era dead code, achievements and cosmetics absent.: A hardcore player has no rank, level, badge or collection to work toward beyond one weekly time.
- [major] Per-mode personal bests are recorded for 4 of 13 modes and displayed nowhere; lifetime sessions/games/flight time are recorded and never shown.: The cheapest 'beat yourself' retention surface is already half-built and invisible.
- [major] Weekly leaderboard promises rewards (tooltip UI) that no system pays; Regional board list is empty.: A visible promise with nothing behind it reads as broken at launch.
- [minor] Payout is placement-only with no participation floor, and in any two-domain match (the common 1-human + AI case) the loser earns 0.: A new player who loses early sees a 4,000 price tag and no path; docs already flagged it (ECONOMY_AND_PRICING.md §2 🟡).
- [minor] Codex has no runtime reader; docs claim a runtime UI loads it.: A collection/discovery meta is designed in data but unreachable.
- [minor] Docs disagree with assets on the weekly pool (10 documented, 8 shipped) and on payouts (ECONOMY_AND_PRICING §1 still says 5 crystals).: Planning built on the docs will mis-size the loop.

### Quick wins
- Decide the progression question explicitly rather than by accident: either delete the quest/hangar gate (and the 'MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)' prefab) and update ECONOMY_TABLES/MENU_PROGRESSION docs to 'everything open', or wire GameModeProgressionService + ProgressionConfig.asset into Bootstrap, add PROFILE to ScreenSwitcher.screens, replace the WildlifeBlitz quest step with a live mode, and fix the claim/hangar flag mismatch (GameModeProgressionService.cs:225 vs :172-203). The first option is a one-day change.
- Give crystals a real sink or make ownership real: either honour SO_Vessel.IsLocked in GameDataSO.ClampVesselToGame / ToyVesselRoster (and lower prices so a locked mode is a 3-5 win goal), or drop vessel prices to ~500-1,000 and add a second sink (e.g. domain trail colour variants) — the current 4,000 buys nothing.
- Add a participation payout (e.g. 25 crystals for finishing, 0 only on quit) in Scoreboard.placementCrystalRewards logic — a two-line change that removes the 'lost = nothing' cliff for the 1-human-vs-AI case.
- Surface the data already recorded: a Profile/Home panel showing crystals, lifetime games/flight time, weekly best and the four stored per-mode bests (PlayerProfileData.Lifecycle, ModeStatsCloudData) — no new persistence needed; add ReportModeResult calls for the nine modes that report nothing.
- Make the weekly leaderboard reward real: read the UGS weekly archive on rollover and credit crystals by rank into PlayerDataService.AddCrystals (or remove the reward tooltip button until it exists).
- Account durability: before Steam EA, at minimum implement SignInWithSteamAsync/LinkWithSteamAsync via UGS Steam identity so the anonymous PlayerId survives a reinstall; failing that, ship an in-menu 'transfer code' export of the session and warn on first boot.
- Fix the catalog/doc drift cheaply: add Rampage and Salvo entries to WeeklyChallengeCatalog.asset (or correct the doc), and delete the stale `winnerCrystalReward: 5` scene overrides.

### Open questions
- Is the absence of GameModeProgressionService from every scene an intentional 'everything open' decision for Early Access, or an unmerged branch (the investor doc says workstream C is 'on a separate branch')? Which is the launch intent?
- Given ClampVesselToGame hands out unowned hulls, what is the intended meaning of vessel ownership at launch — a choice in multi-vessel modes only, or a real gate?
- Will Steam identity (UGS Steam sign-in or Steam Cloud) ship before or after EA launch, and is silent progress loss on reinstall an accepted launch risk?
- Are weekly leaderboard rank rewards scoped for EA, and does the UGS dashboard already have the weekly reset + archive + ascending sort configured (code cannot verify)?
- Is a participation/loss payout still off the table (ECONOMY_TABLES Table 2 says 'Losing payout: None') now that the Hangar has no quest gate to bank crystals toward?
- Should the Scarab (unlocked, priced 100, absent from the Hangar list) appear in the Hangar, and should Grizzly/Termite (locked, priced, not playable) be dropped from SO_Classlist_All?
- Is the Codex meant to ship as a player-facing screen in EA, and is discovery/collection progression a candidate for the missing long-term hook?


## DIMENSION: Presentation, readability and marketability

The engineering under the screen is unusually deliberate: the readability layer is written as PLATFORM LAWS bound in one place (VesselController.cs:163-167 binds the prism occlusion corridor, the speed tunnel and the vessel vision band under IsLocalPilot), the prism palette is measured rather than eyeballed (Docs/PALETTE.md, OriginalColorSetSO.asset), every vessel gets element flowers, tails/jets and the four-card ability lockup structurally, and the arcade card shows the real mode playing live instead of a video. The arcade grid a player sees is the 13 shipped multiplayer modes (AppManager.prefab injects OrganicRematchGames.asset; Menu_Main has 12 GameCard slots) — the 20 legacy SO_ArcadeGame assets with missing scenes are not in that list, so the grid does not ship dead cards.

But almost none of it has been SEEN. Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md is 1,373 lines of unrun tests; the only pass on record is "the project compiles". Palette tiers were verified by simulation only ("the engine has never rendered any of it", line 424), the occlusion corridor carries a magenta-on-load risk, the speed tunnel, vision band, charge-crystal shader and ability row are all unverified, and a Windows IL2CPP player has never been confirmed to reach the menu (P0). Marketability assets are absent from the repo entirely: no capsules, no screenshots, no trailer, and the investor checkpoint flags them as the tightest constraint (13 person-days on one contributor). Audio is thin: one music event for the whole game, boost is silent, 9 of 12 hulls have no engine loop, and creature/crystal emitters ignore the SFX slider until the FMOD VCAs are routed. Accessibility rows (colourblind, subtitles, reduce flashing) are stored and read by nothing — the checkpoint calls them complete; the code says otherwise. The UI redesign has one of eight tasks done; the canvas is still mid-migration from 800×450 with 1,734 identical overrides per scene, six font families and a filmstrip that still carries two disabled screens. FTUE, first-launch flow, dialogue and the codex UI are all built-but-unreachable, so nothing teaches a new player what mass, cells or the nucleus mean beyond a hex gauge in the corner.

Would a 30-second trailer explain itself? The world would: neon conserved-mass ribbons in three domain colours, living cells, eleven silhouettes each flying differently — and the vision band, tails and the domain top bar make teams legible at arena range. What a trailer cannot show is a score you understand without the goal stack, or sound that matches the picture. The gap is not art direction; it is verification, audio, and onboarding.

### Capabilities
- [LIVE] Domain palette and prism tier readability (plain / shielded / super-shielded / danger): Colour is information here: tier is form + palette, crystal colour = who may collect, element = shape. Jade renders teal/blue (TrailHighlightColor 0.05,0.75,0.71), which is why players report 'I got blue' — a naming/readability trap the docs already record. Shielded mass carries gameplay meaning (never food), so tier legibility is load-bearing. Never rendered in engine after the last three palette PRs; a stale Library reimport is the first thing to check.
  - evidence: Assets/_SO_Assets/Color Palettes/OriginalColorSetSO.asset:18-24,40-46,62-68 (per-domain base/rim pairs for three tiers), :106-109 (BrightCTA lime, Danger rim); Docs/PALETTE.md §1 (this asset is the only wired colour set; CosmicWave/Pastel/Default are unwired), §2 (_DarkColor = base face, _BrightColor = fresnel rim), §4.0 (rim brighter than base on every tier×domain, ΔL* measured); Assets/_Scripts/UI/DomainVolumeHexGraphic.cs:108-114 and DomainVolumeIndicator.cs:332-351 (UI domain colours resolved from the same SO_ColorSet at runtime); Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:421-457 QA-PALETTE-SHIELDED ⬜ — 'Colorimetry verified by simulation only — the engine has never rendered any of it'
  - hardcore fit: Tier legibility at speed decides whether a pilot can read food vs armour vs trap; measured contrast gives competitive consistency across domains. | casual fit: Three saturated domain colours plus lime 'free pickup' is an easy first read, but the Jade-is-teal naming will confuse until a colour name matches the hue.
- [PARTIAL] Post-processing look (bloom, HDR): The neon look exists, but the bloom clamp at 0.5 means extra bloom is bought only with bright AREA, not intensity — every HDR channel above 0.5 is wasted authoring. No shadow/bloom toggles are exposed; quality presets own it.
  - evidence: Assets/_Graphics/GamePlay PostProcessing Profile.asset:85-96 (threshold 0.2, intensity 2.5, clamp 0.5 overridden); Docs/PALETTE.md:106-119 ('Bloom is CLAMPED… brightness above the clamp is a dead dial'; 56 of 86 authored colours bloom identically); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Managers/PostProcessingManager.cs (Panini override used by the speed tunnel)
  - hardcore fit: Consistent bloom across tiers avoids one domain 'blowing out' and reading as an unfair beacon. | casual fit: The glow is the game's visual hook; the clamp caps how much of it a screenshot can carry.
- [LIVE] Vessel vision band (find other pilots at arena range): Halo-Infinite-style team-coloured silhouette, graded both edges, domain colour from GetDomainSignalColor. Hand-authored HLSL, spliced into the shipped graph; no QA item exists for it and it has not been verified in play. No replay/broadcast hold exists (local ship is never marked).
  - evidence: Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/VesselController.cs:167,295 (VesselVisionShading.SetLocalVessel under IsLocalPilot in Initialize and ChangePlayer); Assets/_Graphics/Materials/Graphs/VesselGraph.shadergraph (6 VesselVisionShading references) + VesselVisionShading.hlsl; Assets/Resources/VesselVisionShadingConfig.asset (near 150→350, plateau to 2000, gone by 3500, 3 cel steps, breakup closes by 900u); Docs/VESSEL_VISION.md §1-§2
  - hardcore fit: Reliable target acquisition at 900-2000u is what makes Joust/Dog Fight playable competitively. | casual fit: Answers 'where is everyone' without a minimap.
- [LIVE] Prism occlusion corridor (ship never hidden by mass): Cone from lens to hull, screen-door SHATTER dither, zero per-prism CPU, all prism transparency now rides the same dither. Ships as a platform law but is P0-unverified; a magenta arena on first launch is the failure mode.
  - evidence: Assets/_Graphics/Materials/Graphs/BlockGraph.shadergraph and ExplodingBlockGraph.shadergraph (12 PrismOcclusion refs each) + PrismOcclusionCorridor.hlsl; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/VesselController.cs:165,293 (PrismOcclusionCorridor.SetTarget under IsLocalPilot); Assets/Resources/PrismOcclusionConfig.asset; Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:109-145 QA-PRISM-OCCLUSION ⬜ P0 — 'an HLSL compile failure turns every prism magenta on load'
  - hardcore fit: Keeps the hull readable inside dense trails at speed — the difference between skill and blind luck in a prism tunnel. | casual fit: Stops the most common 'I can't see my ship' complaint for free.
- [LIVE] Speed tunnel camera feel (FOV narrows + Panini relaxes with speed): Absolute mapping: the same speed looks the same on any hull, so a faster vessel visibly sits deeper in the tunnel. Unverified since the per-vessel controller was deleted (PR #668).
  - evidence: Assets/Resources/SpeedTunnelConfig.asset (minEffectSpeed 70, maxEffectSpeed 280, fovDrop 25, paniniDrop 0.5, responsiveness 12); Assets/_Scripts/Utility/VesselSpeedTunnel.cs; VesselController.cs:166,294 (single static driver bound per local pilot); Docs/SPEED_TUNNEL.md §1-§2 (absolute fleet-wide mapping, SetSuppressed only for the replay camera); Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:146-166 QA-SPEED-TUNNEL ⬜
  - hardcore fit: Speed is legible without a speedometer; boost timing reads on screen. | casual fit: Instant sense of 'going fast' — the cheapest trailer beat the game has.
- [PARTIAL] Continuity-of-existence prism animation (bloom in / wither out, GPU-clocked): Trails, flora, fauna and deaths animate off the shader clock; the Skim Race track and Wanderway scenes are the known snap. Visible to a trailer as prisms appearing instantly on the flagship race mode.
  - evidence: Docs/PRISM_ANIMATION.md:11 (STRICT MODE, no CPU fallback), :329 (environment trail lay: grow-in stamps still ❌ remaining); Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:168-186 QA-PRISM-CLOCK-ENV-SNAP 🟡 — SegmentSpawner track prisms 'pop into existence' and log [PrismClock] STRICT MODE; Assets/_Graphics/Materials/Graphs/PrismClockAnimation.hlsl
  - hardcore fit: Deterministic timing; nothing visual costs CPU in a 50k-prism fight. | casual fit: The 'living' feel is the pitch; a snapping track undercuts it on the very mode most likely to be filmed.
- [LIVE] Vessel tails and jets (identification at range, own-thrust readout): Domain-tinted beacon behind the camera plus engine plumes; the skyburst missile carries a tail too. Not audited in play.

  - hardcore fit: Whose missile/whose ship at range, without a HUD tag. | casual fit: Streamer-friendly: a screenshot shows who is where.
- [PARTIAL] Vessel model fidelity and elemental hull morphs: Manta family, Sparrow, Serpent, Squirrel and Scarab (procedural) morph with element level; Dolphin/Urchin/Rhino cannot until a hands-on rig swap; Grizzly has no labelled shapes. CLAUDE.md's 'Rhino wires a literal placeholder' is contradicted by VESSEL_CONSTRUCTION.md:67-73 (a .meta remap false positive) — code/doc audit wins. Two of the marquee hulls ship as '_Test' art.

- [PARTIAL] Vessel HUD: ability lockup (four totem cards), control chips, cooldown veil: Structural and derived (a wrong chip is impossible), but only Squirrel/Sparrow/Dolphin/Scarab show four real abilities; Manta/Rhino/Serpent show four locked slots and the Rhino lost its status cluster (debuff timer, slowed count) to the root sweep. PlayStation players see Xbox A/B letters on the Sparrow. Unverified in play.

What ships is the mobile-era menu scaled ×2.4 with two greyed filmstrip slots, a modal Arcade, and a Home/Hangar/Profile spine.
