### Strengths
- A world that photographs: procedural cell environments (34-69k prisms), grown flora, sharks/worm colonies, neon conserved-mass ribbons in three domain colours — the trailer subject already exists in the build
- Readability is engineered as un-authorable platform laws (occlusion corridor, speed tunnel, vision band) bound in one method (VesselController.cs:163-167), so no vessel or mode can ship without them
- Measured, documented palette contract (PALETTE.md) — tier and crystal colours carry gameplay meaning and are derived from one SO_ColorSet with tests and generators
- Derived HUD: ability lockup, control chips and the goal stack are computed from data (ability maps, ScoringRuleSO, ObjectiveIconSet), so labels cannot be wrong and every hull inherits the row
- The arcade card plays the real mode live and lets you tap in — the strongest self-explaining surface in the game
- Connecting panel shows the arena being built with a monotonic bar and a pilot roster; end-game reveal has tone (slang pool) and team framing
- Arcade grid is clean: the injected list is the 13 shipped modes, every scene on disk; dead legacy cards are not shown
- Style Foundation v0.3.2 is a real, design-approved spec with a token SO and a literal audit — the redesign has a map even if the road is unbuilt

### Gaps
- [critical] No store-page assets exist (capsules ×5, ≥5 screenshots, trailer) and the one contributor is the schedule's tightest constraint: A Coming Soon page cannot go live without capsules, and a page without a trailer converts poorly; wishlists cannot start compounding until this lands
- [critical] The entire readability layer is unverified in engine — palette tiers, occlusion corridor (magenta-on-load risk), speed tunnel, vision band, charge crystal shader, ability row — and no Windows player build is confirmed: Hand-authored HLSL and prefab YAML from remote sessions; a first-boot magenta arena or a linker failure would be the first thing a playtester sees
- [major] No onboarding: FTUE, first-launch flow, dialogue and codex UI are all built but unreachable; the core loop (mass → cell → nucleus control) is explained only by an unlabelled hex gauge: The tagline targets casual players; the first five minutes have no guidance and the differentiating mechanic is invisible
- [major] Audio would read as silence in a trailer: one music event for the whole game, silent boost, no engine loop on 9 of 12 hulls, fauna/crystal loops ignore the SFX slider until VCAs are routed: Speed, abilities and the living world have no audible identity; the checkpoint's C6 audio pass has not started
- [major] UI redesign is 1 of 8 tasks done; canvas migration from 800×450 unfinished; six font families; 1,734 identical overrides per game scene; two disabled screens still in the menu filmstrip with no coming-soon treatment: First impression on a PC monitor is a scaled phone UI with greyed nav links; every prefab edit is silently masked by scene overrides
- [major] Accessibility rows are decoys: colourblind, subtitles, subtitle scale, reduce flashing, camera shake are stored and read by nothing, while the investor checkpoint reports them complete: In a game whose information channel is colour, a non-functional colourblind mode is a refund and a review; the doc/code disagreement is a credibility risk
- [major] Vessel art fidelity uneven: Dolphin and Urchin ship _Test part-mesh FBXs with no element morphs; Manta family draws two coincident hulls; Manta/Rhino/Serpent HUDs show four locked cards and the Rhino lost its status readouts; Urchin/Grizzly have no HUD: Two marquee hulls (Dolphin is the Rampage/Bends hull) look like placeholders in close-ups; three flyable hulls read as unfinished on the HUD
- [minor] Objective cues incomplete: no off-screen arrow in 5 of 12 modes, arrow is hardcoded green rather than domain-tinted, SkimRace overtake/leader toasts never fire, Joust toast feed rect is off-screen, CORE-fork modes have no toast feed: Mid-match narrative and 'go here' cues are what keep a casual player oriented
- [minor] Known visual defects: Skim Race track prisms snap in (STRICT MODE errors), Dolphin cone flash does not scale, dead subtrees in GameOverPanel, Unity logo splash on, PlayStation players see Xbox A/B on the Sparrow: Each is visible in exactly the footage a trailer would use (the flagship race track, the Dolphin blast)
- [minor] No performance floor or load-time numbers on a min-spec machine; hardware requirements cannot be entered on the store page until D1 runs; bloom clamp at 0.5 makes half the authored colours a dead dial: CPU-bound prism sim with 50-70k-prism arenas and no published target is a review risk on a $15 EA
- [minor] Localisation absent and mis-described ('strings continue to go into tables' — no tables exist): Accepted for EA, but the retrofit cost grows with every prefab-authored string

### Quick wins
- One editor afternoon running the P0/P1 visual QA gates (occlusion magenta check, palette tiers, speed tunnel, ability row, charge crystal) converts the whole readability layer from 'unverified' to 'seen' — Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md already scripts each test step by step
- Capture the 5+ store screenshots now from the existing Recording Studio / PhotoBooth tool scenes and the Cell Selector worlds (Atlantis, Hesperides, Boneyard); wire CaptureScreenShot.cs to a hotkey in a dev build — under a day
- Remove or wire the decoy accessibility rows: either add a colourblind post-process consumer and route subtitles to a stub, or hide the rows until they do something; a setting that lies is worse than none
- FMOD C1+C2 (route the two VCAs, remove the Boost Activate loop region) restores boost sound and slider control over every emitter with no C#; add ShipAudioController with the existing engine event to the nine silent hulls (inspector only)
- Place RaceRankToastDriver in MinigameSkimRace, fix the Joust NotificationUI rect, and author idle-hint entries for every mode in the GameToastConfig_* assets — copy is data, no code
- Tint ObjectiveArrowGraphic from SO_ColorSet (T8 flag F5) and add the Bends provider case to MiniGameHUD.CreateObjectiveProviderForGameMode so the arrow says whose objective and covers the Dolphin duel
- Hide or coming-soon the ARK/PORT nav links (checkpoint C4), turn off the Unity splash if the licence allows, set defaultScreen to native, and bump bundleVersion off 0.2.0 before any playtest build
- Re-enable HomeScreen.FirstAppLaunchExperience against the existing FTUE_Canvas prefab and the four authored step handlers for a minimal first-launch prompt (freestyle → arcade) — the code path is built and commented out
- Author ModeControlsLibrary rows (data-only) so the launch panel shows a stick/throttle primer for each hull family, using the ControlGlyphSet that already draws chips
- Record one cold-boot and one menu-to-first-frame timing per mode on the dev machine and publish them in Docs — the D2 matrix needs numbers before it needs fixes

### Open questions
- Has a Windows IL2CPP player ever been built and played through a full match on a GTX-1060-class machine? The repo has no artefact, no Windows build profile, and QA-BUILD-WINDOWS-PLAYER is unchecked
- Does capsule/key art or trailer footage exist outside version control (the style-guide source art does), or is Workstream E genuinely starting from zero?
- After a fresh Library reimport, do the shipped palette tiers (gold shielded sand/cream, danger rim over shielded base) actually read as the simulation predicts?
- Which modes does GameModeProgressionService lock at first boot — i.e. what does a brand-new player actually see in the 12-slot arcade grid?
- Is the Unity licence Pro (so the 'Made with Unity' splash can be disabled), and where are the FMOD banks placed for the Steam depot (none under Assets/StreamingAssets in this checkout)?
- Will the redesign (T2-T8) land before EA, or does the mobile-era menu ship with the disabled ARK/PORT slots visible?
- Is the strobe risk of the screen-door dither at speed acceptable for photosensitive players, given reduce-flashing has no consumer?
- Which name/hue pairing ships for Jade — the teal/blue palette or the cyan 00D4FF the style guide names — since players already report 'I picked gold and got blue'?


## DIMENSION: Platform and tech readiness

The platform story is stronger than the docs admit and weaker than the investor checkpoint claims. Contrary to Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §0 ("Authored, not running"), CI is live: a self-hosted Windows runner (`cosmic-build-win`) compiles the project and runs the edit-mode suite on every push to bleeding-edge (183 guard runs, last green 2026-09-03), a weekly IL2CPP player build exists (last green 2026-09-01, 26 min), and the promotion/tagging workflows fire on schedule. What a player gets today is a Windows x64 IL2CPP build produced by a scripted, versioned pipeline (`CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.cs`), with consent-gated UGS+PostHog analytics (real PostHog key committed), consent-gated Unity Cloud Diagnostics crash capture, UGS anonymous auth with Cloud Save plus a local last-known-good cache, a loopback-host offline mode, and Relay-backed parties. No Steamworks SDK of any kind is integrated, by decision (`Tools/Steam/README.md`; `SignInWithSteamAsync` is a `Task.CompletedTask` stub), and SteamPipe upload cannot run because no app id exists anywhere in the repo.

Three things block shipping fast and often during EA. First, build identity: every build is `0.2.0` (`ProjectSettings.asset:150`, `buildNumber` blank), the commit SHA lands only in `build_manifest.txt`, and crash reports stamp `Application.version` — a player bug report cannot be mapped to a commit. Second, verification debt: CI proves the code compiles and an exe appears, but nothing boots the player; `Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md` holds 64 untested items including P0 gates (Windows player boots, scoring mirror, occlusion shader) with exactly one QA session filed, and trunk commits routinely state "NOT compiled in Unity and NOT play-tested" (159 of 218 commits since Aug 1 are Claude-authored). Third, the compile monolith: 1,481 files / ~235k lines in `Assembly-CSharp`, phase 2 of the split blocked, wall-clock never measured.

Later platforms cost real money. Console readiness is absent (zero console defines, anonymous-only identity, no rebinding). Steam Deck/Linux has a Linux build profile, cross-toolchain and FMOD `.so`s in-tree but no verification, and Windows is pinned to DX12-only (`m_APIs: 12000000`, `m_Automatic: 0`). Mobile is rotting rather than retired: Android/iOS settings, touch input and Adaptive Performance package remain, but AP has zero code references, the Ads resolver entry is stale after Ads removal, and no CI builds it. Multiplayer scale is unmeasured (ROADMAP "Scale & cost story" unchecked), 3–4-player party reliability is an open bug (B5), and host migration is absent. Legal blockers (privacy policy URL empty, PostHog erasure only partial) are cheap but unstarted.

### Capabilities
- [LIVE] Scripted Windows x64 IL2CPP build: Build script is the source of truth and CI-proven. Managed stripping deliberately untouched (Reflex/Netcode/Newtonsoft reflection) so player size is unoptimised. The only in-editor Build Profile asset is Linux (Assets/Settings/Build Profiles/CS Linux build profile.asset); Windows has none.

Whether a red edit-mode test blocks depends on the repo variable UNITY_TESTS_BLOCKING, which is not visible from the checkout. ### Strengths
- A scripted, versioned Windows IL2CPP build (CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.cs) that CI has actually produced (2026-09-01, 26 min warm), with a self-hosted runner compiling and running edit-mode tests on every trunk push — better than Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md admits.
- Release model (bleeding-edge → development → master, robot-owned build/* snapshots, weekly internal tags, 3-weekly test promotion) is live and one-directional by construction.
- Consent-gated telemetry is real: UGS + PostHog sinks behind a self-built COPPA age gate and opt-in dialog that cannot be un-wired (AppManager.cs:254), with a committed EU project key and export/backfill scripts.
- Crash capture is correctly privacy-postured (disarmed before scene load, armed only on consent, no player id).
- Cloud Save was consolidated with SchemaVersion on every record and a local last-known-good cache; offline play is a real loopback host rather than a code-path fork.
- Static gates that need no Unity (validate_project.py, check_conditional_compilation.py, ten shader-wiring checks) catch the historically recurring release-build breakers cheaply.
- Steam delivery is already scripted (SteamPipe templates, depot exclusions, branch convention) so arming it is a config change once the app id exists.
- ~1,300 edit-mode tests exist and are exercised in CI; the QA loop tooling (backlog generator, submit/apply scripts, editor window) is ready to absorb testers.

### Gaps
- [critical] No Steamworks app id — SteamPipe upload, overlay verification, Steam Input config and the 30-day Steam Direct clock are all blocked on an off-repo business step: Nothing can be delivered to any Steam branch until A2 is done; every day it slips is a day of launch slip regardless of engineering.
- [critical] Builds have no identity: every build reports 0.2.0, buildNumber is blank, crash metadata and the settings panel show the same string: During EA a bug report cannot be tied to a commit, crash reports cannot be grouped by build, and 'is this fixed in your build' is unanswerable — this defeats the whole point of shipping often.
- [critical] Untested-development debt: 64 QA items including every P0 gate never run, one QA session filed, commits routinely land 'NOT compiled in Unity and NOT play-tested', and CI never launches the player it builds: CI proves compilation, not that the game boots or plays; with most commits agent-authored and unplayed, the first real integration test of a week's work is the player base.
- [major] Player identity is a device-bound anonymous UGS session with no linking or recovery path (Steam auth is a stub): A reinstall, new PC or cleared profile silently creates a new account, losing vessels, progression, weekly-challenge standing and the friends graph — with no support tooling to merge. On Steam this will generate refund-adjacent tickets.
- [major] Legal disclosures unfinished: privacy policy not hosted (URL empty), DATA_ARCHITECTURE §8 BLOCKING items open, PostHog erasure only partial: A Steam store page requires a privacy policy URL; sending display names to PostHog without the disclosure and a working erasure path is a GDPR exposure the docs themselves flag as release-blocking.
- [major] Multiplayer at 3-4 players is unproven (B5 open), no host migration or rejoin, Relay/Lobby cost and CCU limits unmeasured, presence discovery capped at one 100-player lobby: The party game's core promise is friends in a room; a launch spike beyond ~100 CCU breaks 'who's online', and a host drop ends the party. Cost of eager per-user Relay allocations is unknown before a paid launch.
- [major] The scheduled IL2CPP tier is flaky for non-code reasons (development branch missing bleeding-edge tools; a cancelled run) and produces no bootable artefact check: The one tier that catches AOT/link failures is red two runs in a row for reasons unrelated to the game, so real failures will be lost in noise; and 'exe exists' is not 'player reaches menu'.
- [major] Assembly-CSharp monolith (1,481 files / ~235k lines) with the split blocked at phase 2 and compile time never measured: Every one-line change recompiles the whole game; this is the standing tax on content velocity for a 12-month EA with weekly internal builds.
- [minor] Mobile path is de-scoped but not retired: touch input, Android/iOS settings, gradle templates, UGS Android target and a stale unity-ads resolver entry persist with no CI and no device QA; Adaptive Performance package has zero code references: Silently rotting platform code adds build risk and misleads docs/pitches ('mobile-first'); either gate it out of the Windows build or keep a compile check for it.
- [minor] Windows graphics API pinned to Direct3D12 only (m_Automatic: 0) with no recorded rationale: DX12-only raises the effective min-spec, complicates Proton/Steam Deck, and removes the DX11 fallback older 1060-class drivers may need — exactly the stated hardware floor.
- [minor] No input rebinding and Steam Input reliance without a Steamworks configuration; PS pads show Xbox face glyphs on the Sparrow: A recurring Steam review complaint class; the 'Steam Input covers it' claim only holds once a Steam Input config is authored post-A2.
- [minor] Documentation drift on platform facts (README: Wwise/Cinemachine; BUILD_AND_DELIVERY: CI 'not running'; CLAUDE.md: fauna client-local vs FaunaNetworkSync; checkpoint: strings 'already in tables'): Anyone planning from the docs will misjudge readiness in both directions.

### Quick wins
- Stamp commit SHA + CI run number into PlayerSettings.bundleVersion (or a Resources TextAsset) inside CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.Run, surface it in GameSettingsPanelController and CrashReportingService metadata — Docs/BRANCHING_AND_RELEASE.md §6 R1 estimates half a day.
- Add a player smoke step to the IL2CPP tier: launch Builds/Windows64/CosmicShore.exe with -logFile, wait for the ApplicationState MainMenu line or a 120 s timeout, fail otherwise — turns 'exe exists' into 'game boots'.
- Make the Tuesday pre-flight run the static tool checks from bleeding-edge's Tools/ (or skip a missing tool with a warning) so branch drift cannot redden the only AOT gate; then set BUILD_PROMOTION_REQUIRES_GREEN=true and confirm UNITY_TESTS_BLOCKING.
- Host the privacy policy template at a public URL and fill Assets/Resources/PrivacyConsentConfig.asset privacyPolicyUrl and GameSettingsPanelController.privacyPolicyUrl (Workstream F1, ~1 day).
- Run one QA session against the P0 block (QA-BUILD-WINDOWS-PLAYER, QA-SCORING-CLIENT-MIRROR, QA-PRISM-OCCLUSION, QA-EDITMODE-TESTS) using the CI-built player; the loop tooling already exists.
- Fire a forced exception from a CI development build and confirm it lands in the Unity Cloud Diagnostics dashboard; record the result in Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §5.
- Add Direct3D11 after Direct3D12 in the Windows graphics API list (or set m_Automatic) and record why DX12-only was chosen if it was deliberate.
- Remove the stale unity-ads line from ProjectSettings/AndroidResolverDependencies.xml, disable UnityPurchasingSettings, and delete Assets/Wwise + Assets/_Scripts/Utility/ChoppingBlock — smaller player, fewer misleading imports.
- Decide account portability for launch: wire UGS 'Sign in with Unity Player Account' (or Steam via UGS once the SDK question is revisited) as a link option; until then add a 'copy my player id' + support path so a lost account is at least identifiable.
- Fix the doc drift in README.md (FMOD, custom cameras) and Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §0 (CI is running) so planning starts from the real state.

### Open questions
- Has the Steamworks partner account been created and the $100 fee paid? No app id, depot id or date exists anywhere in the repo, and the 30-day clock is the launch gate.
- Are the repo variables UNITY_TESTS_BLOCKING and BUILD_PROMOTION_REQUIRES_GREEN set? The guard is green, but whether a red edit-mode test would block cannot be read from the checkout.
- Why is Windows pinned to Direct3D12 only (ProjectSettings.asset:577-579)? Deliberate for the prism renderer, or an accident that raises min-spec and hurts Deck/Proton?
- Is Cloud Diagnostics enabled in the Unity dashboard, and has any crash from a real build ever appeared there?
- Are all 27 UGSKeys events (and their parameters) declared in the UGS Event Manager? Undeclared events are silently discarded (AnalyticsServiceFacade.cs:22-24).
- Is the UGS Android build target still building from build/android, and is mobile meant to survive EA or be retired? Today it is neither maintained nor removed.
- What is the projected Relay + Lobby cost at 200 / 500 / 1,000 CCU under eager per-user allocations, and what replaces the single 100-player presence lobby past that?
- Who owns QA throughput? One session (one verdict) in three weeks against a 64-item backlog is the binding constraint on shipping often.
- What is the account-identity plan for Steam players — accept device-bound anonymous accounts for EA, or add a linking provider before launch?
- Is `development` kept in step with bleeding-edge's Tools/ directory? The 2026-09-01 pre-flight went red because it was not.


## VERIFIER CORRECTIONS (adversarial second pass)

### Verdict
The audit is unusually reliable: 25 of 26 statuses hold on re-check, the architectural conclusion is right, and most line cites land within a line or two. The three most important corrections are, first, that OFFLINE MODE'S RECONNECT AFFORDANCE IS ACTUALLY PLACED — `OnlineStatusIndicator` is itself a Button that injects `ReconnectService` and calls `ReconnectAsync()`/`GoOfflineAsync()` through a `ConfirmQuestionBar`, and it, the question bar and `OfflineUIGate` are all instanced in Menu_Main.unity; only the redundant `ReconnectButton` is unplaced, so the "no way back online except a restart" gap should drop from major to minor, leaving only the unrun editor verification (the auditor followed `Docs/OFFLINE_MODE.md:394`, which the scene YAML contradicts — code wins). Second, the auditor MISSED the game's one genuine multiplayer synchronisation feature: `Player.NetArenaReady` + `ReportArenaReady_ServerRpc` + `ConnectingPlayerRoster` hold every match at the connecting panel, with a greyed→lit chip per human pilot and a 45 s cap, live in 12 of the 13 shipped scenes — the only fairness guarantee in the build and the party's only shared waiting room; alongside it, party persistence between matches (host-only Play Again re-runs the card for everyone; a client gets Leave Lobby) is live and unlisted. Third, "public matchmaking is dead" is correct but the supporting evidence is not — three of the four MultiplayerSetup.prefab scenes ARE `enabled: 1` in EditorBuildSettings and ship in the player; they simply have no card, and the real reason strangers can never meet is the eager-Relay early return at `MultiplayerSetup.cs:299-304` plus `IsPrivate = true` at `PartySessionService.cs:172-178`. Four smaller misses are worth folding in — no reporting/mute/moderation surface of any kind (only a kick), painting share on Windows opens a local HTML in a browser rather than sharing anything, all purely co-operative cards are dead (`ArcadeGameCoOpWildlifeBlitz` is in zero game lists), and there are no world-space nameplates so friends are told apart by domain colour alone. Two small severity notes: `Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md` lists B6 as 🔴 not 🟡, and the four Wildlife Liberation shark configs now carry `NetworkSynced: 1`, which answers one of the auditor's open questions from the assets and makes the B16 doc line stale.
- CORRECTION Offline mode (local loopback host, cached profile) — specifically the "Reconnect UI is unplaced / no way back online" claim: partial (with a MAJOR gap: "its Reconnect button is placed in no scene or prefab", "the way back online is missing from the UI", "the only way back online may be a restart") -> partial — but the reconnect affordance IS placed and wired; the gap is minor, not major: The auditor followed a stale doc line. `ReconnectButton` is a redundant second affordance; the SHIPPED path back online is the lamp itself — one tap, a GO ONLINE? confirmation, then `ReconnectService.ReconnectAsync()`, with the whole offline surface (gate + question bar + lamp) already instanced in Menu_Main. What genuinely remains open is only that `Docs/OFFLINE_MODE_SUMMARY.md:58` still says "**Not editor-verified**" — i.e. the human play-test gate, not the wiring. Downgrade the gap from major to minor and reword it as "offline mode is wired but unverified in the editor". CODE/ASSETS WIN; the doc is stale.)

The docs say code-complete, B16 says every shipped config is off, and 4 assets now say on") -> partial — but the question is answerable from the assets: 4 shark configs ship with server-authoritative sync ON, everything else per-peer: Not a status flip — the dimension-level conclusion ("ecology is per-peer in every mode but one") is correct. But this belongs in `capabilities` as a settled fact, not in `open_questions`: the release build ships exactly one species (Wildlife Liberation's sharks, all four element variants) under server authority, and every other flora/fauna config in the game is simulated independently on each peer. - ADDITION [live] Synchronized arena-load gate + live pilot roster ("who is still loading"): Entirely absent from the auditor's inventory, and it is the single most player-visible piece of multiplayer synchronisation the game has: every peer builds the arena independently off its own clock, so the match holds at the connecting panel until every HUMAN pilot reports ready, with one greyed→lit chip per human (AI are excluded because a chip that can never change reads as a player who never arrives). Capped at 45 s. HARDCORE: nobody starts a race mid-load, which is a real fairness guarantee — the one integrity property this build actually has. CASUAL: the group can see whose machine everyone is waiting on instead of staring at a frozen screen; it is also the only place a party sees each other's avatars between menu and match. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Player/Player.cs:45 — `public NetworkVariable<bool> NetArenaReady = new(false, ReadPermission.Everyone, WritePermission.Server);`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Player/Player.cs:151-159 — `ReportArenaReady()` → `ReportArenaReady_ServerRpc()` (the 4th sibling of the owner-detects/server-records round trip); :149 `IsArenaReady => !IsSpawned || NetArenaReady.Value`; :468 and :638 set it true by construction for AI; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/ConnectingPlayerRoster.cs:127 — `ReportLocalReady() => gameData?.LocalPlayer?.ReportArenaReady();`; Assets/_Scripts/UI/ConnectingPanelController.cs:77 (pilot-chip row), :85 `peerWaitTimeoutSeconds = 45f`, :289-295 (releases LOUD on timeout); Assets/_Scripts/UI/MiniGameHUD.cs:523 — the no-panel branch also reports, so a peer's panel is never waiting on a machine that will never answer; ConnectingPanelController guid → 12 of the 13 shipped scenes (all but Maelstrom.unity, which is the meta hub); ConnectingPlayerRoster lives in Assets/_Prefabs/UI Elements/In Game/ConnectingPanel.prefab)
- ADDITION [live] Party persistence between matches (host Play Again replays everyone; client Leave Lobby): The auditor's summary implies the party survives a match ("return → re-invite") but never lists the mechanism, and never lists LEAVE PARTY as a capability at all. The Relay party session is torn down only by an explicit leave or a transport failure, so a party genuinely persists across game→menu→game with no re-invite needed, and one host click re-runs the same card for everybody. HARDCORE: back-to-back rematches with the same four people, no lobby rebuild between rounds — the closest thing to a scrim loop the game has. CASUAL: the 'again?' button exists, it is just host-only, so a guest who wants a rematch has to ask out-of-band (no chat, no emote — see the comms gap). (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Scoreboard.cs:661-687 — `OnPlayAgainButtonPressed()`: host-only (`if (nm == null || !nm.IsServer) return`), "A host click forces everyone to replay through the controller's server-authoritative reset pipeline" → `gameController.RequestReplay()`; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Scoreboard.cs:239 — `leaveLobbyButton.SetActive(isClient)`; :729-741 `OnLeaveLobbyButtonPressed()` → `PartyInviteController.Instance.LeavePartyAndReturnToMenuAsync()`; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/ArcadeLobbyList.cs:361 — `await service.LeavePartyAsync();` (leave from the party panel in Menu_Main); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/PartyInviteController.cs:275 `LeavePartyAndReturnToMenuAsync()`; HostConnectionService.cs:841 `LeavePartyAsync()`, :1174 `LeavePartySessionAsync()`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/Maelstrom/MaelstromSceneView.cs:429 — the Maelstrom results screen uses the same leave path)
- ADDITION [partial] In-match player identification (who is who, mid-flight): A social-legibility capability the inventory omits. Names reach the player through the toast feed and the per-player score cards, but the new top bar shows avatars without names and nothing labels a ship in the world — the vessel vision band marks a hull in its DOMAIN colour, which is a team signal, not an identity. HARDCORE: in a 2v2 you cannot tell which of the two enemy hulls is the good player; there is no way to focus a specific opponent. CASUAL: you cannot tell which ship is your friend versus the AI wearing the same domain colour — in a party game with mixed human/AI rosters this is the friction that makes 'where are you?' the most common thing said over Discord. On the Steam target, flying the painting toy's SHARE gate writes a self-contained WebGL HTML reconstruction to disk and opens it in the player's browser — it does NOT post anywhere, produce a link, or upload anything, so 'sharing' means the player manually attaching a local file. HARDCORE: irrelevant. CASUAL: this is the one thing a player could show a friend who does not own the game, and on Windows it stops one step short of being shareable — no screenshot capture, no clip, no link. For a $15 paid EA with no public matchmaking, the missing shareable artefact is a real acquisition gap. Today a player who is griefed in a party can be KICKED by the host and nothing else: they cannot be blocked (no UI over a working backend), cannot be reported (no code path at all), and cannot be muted (no comms to mute). HARDCORE: no recourse against a deliberate thrower in a mode where a host quit already voids the match. CASUAL: acceptable while the game is friends-only and invite-only — it becomes a Steam-review liability the day any public join path ships, which is exactly the quick win the auditor recommends first. Ordering matters: public matchmaking must not ship ahead of block+report. HARDCORE: no neutral-server option means no credible competitive integrity is reachable without an architecture change, not a feature addition. CASUAL: peer hosting is the right call at this scale and cost — nothing to fix here for EA, but it caps how far the competitive story can go. - ADDITION [dead] Co-op / PvE-together cards (2v2 vs AI, Co-op Wildlife Blitz): The inventory treats "team modes" as covered but never states that every purely CO-OPERATIVE card is unreachable. All 13 shipped cards are competitive domain races; humans on the same domain are teammates, which is co-op only in the sense that you share a score. CASUAL: this is a real shape gap for a party game — there is no 'us against the game' card, which is usually the lowest-friction way to bring a nervous friend in. Two such cards were built and are one game-list entry away from returning; note ArcadeGameMultiplayer2v2CoOpVsAI's scene is also one of the four still carrying the dead MultiplayerSetup matchmaking prefab, so reviving it re-enters that code path. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameCoOpWildlifeBlitz.asset (guid b1dc8a89fc726884fb0738de3ba00fc6) → referenced by ZERO game lists under Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/GameLists/; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameMultiplayer2v2CoOpVsAI.asset — not in OrganicRematchGames.asset (the 13-card shipped roster resolved above); Assets/_Scenes/Multiplayer Scenes/MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp.unity is absent from ProjectSettings/EditorBuildSettings.asset entirely; ArcadeGameMultiplayer2v2CoOpVsAI.unity is `enabled: 1` but has no card; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/ — CoOpWildlifeBlitzMiniGame.cs still compiles)

### Verdict
The audit is directionally right about the thing that matters most and unreliable about what is actually on screen. Its central finding survives adversarial checking and is stronger than stated: there is no teaching layer at all — I resolved every FTUE script guid against every scene and prefab (all NONE except a Captain VO prefab that is itself unreferenced), confirmed `GameModeProgressionService` exists only inside a "(DELETE LATER)" migration prefab that nothing instantiates, and confirmed the Quest Graph Editor the QA backlog references (PR #633) is absent from the tree entirely — so the guided progression the README, CLAUDE.md, MENU_PROGRESSION_AND_IAP.md and the investor checkpoint all describe does not run, and every card and intensity is open on boot. But the inventory under-credits the shipped build in ways that would distort a market-gap read. The three most important corrections: (1) **there IS a fully wired PC options panel** — SettingsModal.prefab populates ~25 slots covering display mode, resolution, frame cap, V-Sync, FOV, quality/AA/texture/upscaling, auto-detect, benchmark, colorblind mode, subtitles and consent, reachable from Home and from pause, so "two input toggles, nothing else" is wrong (rebinding alone is genuinely absent); (2) **the onboarding funnel is not blind** — `game_started` (UGSKeys.cs:34, facade:522) and `menu_ready` with `is_first` + `seconds_since_launch` (facade:764-773, called from MainMenuController:193) already make time-to-first-match derivable; and (3) **a new player can fly all eight implemented hulls for free on day one** via the freestyle Vessel Changer, whose roster (ToyVesselRoster.cs:24-49) has no ownership filter — which reframes the multigenre pitch from "locked behind 20 wins" to "shipped but undiscoverable". Two further claims are backwards: the specific keyboard-chip defect (no vessel binds Q, and E is a throttle key, so the four authored labels cover every live binding), and "only Joust has an idle hint" (Scarab Scramble ships two hints that teach its entire loop and is wired into the toast library). The auditor also missed a first-run age-gate-and-consent dialog that AppManager builds before the Authentication scene, a display-name validator that can reject the one thing a new player must type, the authored end-game reveal and 200-crystal payout, and the fact that nobody has ever verified a Windows IL2CPP player reaching the main menu (QA-BUILD-WINDOWS-PLAYER ⬜) on a Windows-only launch. Its QA numbers are also wrong: one verdict has ever been submitted, and it passed. Net: trust the "nothing teaches the game" conclusion; re-verify every "absent" before building a gap analysis on it.
- CORRECTION HUD control chips / ability lockup hints — "only four keyboard glyph labels exist so the Q/E chips draw blank": partial (with the stated defect: Q/E chips blank on keyboard) -> partial, but the stated defect is wrong — keyboard chip coverage is COMPLETE for every ability any vessel binds today: The real gap is different and worth stating correctly: 4 of 8 vessels (Manta, Rhino, Serpent, Urchin) bind 0/4 icons because their ability maps are '(open design slot)' — a design gap, not a glyph-asset gap. The auditor's remediation ('add keyboardLabel entries for Q, E, WASD, P;L\'') would add dead data. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/InputDeviceIconSetSwitcher.cs:43-54 — HintBinding enum: PadButtonSouth=1, PadButtonEast=3, PadLeftTrigger=7, PadRightTrigger=8, KeyQ=24, KeyE=25; Assets/Resources/ControlGlyphSet.asset — entries are bindings 7/8/1/3 with keyboardLabel LSHIFT / RSHIFT / SPACE / R; Assets/_Scripts/ScriptableObjects/ControlGlyphSetSO.cs:79-86 (`For`) falls back to InputHintBindingMap.Canonical, and InputHintBindingMap.cs:80-87 maps KeyLeftShift→PadLeftTrigger, KeyRightShift→PadRightTrigger, KeySpace→PadButtonSouth, KeyR→PadButtonEast; Every shipped ability map uses only Input 0/1/2/6/7/11/12 (Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/*.asset). NO vessel binds Button3Action(8)→KeyQ; InputHintBindingMap.cs:57-58 says so in comment: "Q is last and is bound by no vessel today"; E is a throttle key (KeyboardInputStrategy), never an ability chip — KeyE=25 is absent from InputHintBindingMap's Map dictionary entirely; Starter vessel: Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Squirrel.asset — Input 0 (passive), 12, 0 (passive), 11 → chips resolve to LSHIFT and RSHIFT; the two passives draw blank BY DESIGN)
- CORRECTION Teaching the core loop / idle hints — "only Joust has an idle hint toast": absent (with "GameToastConfig_Joust.asset is the only idle hint") -> partial — three instructional idle hints ship across two modes, and Scarab Scramble's two teach its whole loop: Two smaller errors in the same claim: GameToastConfig_Scurry.asset is `toasts: []` (empty, not "event toasts only"), and SIX configs exist, not four. Twelve of ~16 modes still have none — the shape of the gap is right, the count is not. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Game Toasts/GameToastConfig_ScarabScramble.asset:63-76 — two entries with isIdleHint: 1: "Follow the marker - fly through the bright crystal and it becomes your ball" and "Roll or bat your ball through any glowing ring - bumping enemy balls is always safe, and a juke dash steals one"; Assets/_SO_Assets/Game Toasts/GameToastLibrary.asset — modeConfigs wires FIVE configs; guid e0705bbc27ab8ca3a0070ad189342ade resolves to GameToastConfig_ScarabScramble.asset.meta; Delivery is live in that scene: NotificationUI.prefab (which carries GameToastController, guid 84f2acde620c45e3ba105870265b147c) is nested in Assets/_Prefabs/GameCanvas-SkimRace.prefab, and MinigameScarabScramble.unity instances that canvas (1806 refs) — CORE/GameCanvas.prefab does NOT nest it; Assets/_Scripts/UI/GameToastSystem/GameToastController.cs:74,116 — CollectIdleHints + idleSeconds gate is live)

The genuine gap is that collection is opt-in behind consent (AnalyticsServiceFacade.cs:95-107 NeedsPrivacyFlow), so first-timer data only exists for players who said yes. ### Verdict
The audit is broadly reliable on code it actually opened — the comeback system, client-trusted networking, dead PlayFab leaderboard, unread cloud stats, stub AI gunner, dead RewindSystem, one-round formats and QA state all verified line-for-line — but it made one arithmetic error, took two doc claims on trust, and missed the two systems that most change the picture for this dimension. The three most important corrections: (1) the comeback rubber-band is real, mandatory and unauthored on five cards, but its magnitude is one element level per unit of deficit, not ten (ElementalComebackSystem.cs:332-334 divides by 10 before SetComebackModifier, whose units are normalized where 1.0 = level 10) — so the auditor's headline example is inverted: in Joust (target 3) the default buys at most +3 levels, while the genuinely saturating modes are the count-metric ones, Skim Race and Scurry, where a ten-crystal deficit pins all four elements at 10 and flips every level-5 upgrade on. (2) A full game-mode quest chain with STAT-GATED intensity unlocks is authored and coded — "Complete Hex Race Intensity 2 under 215 seconds" unlocks intensity 3 (GameModeQuest_SkimRace.asset, GameModeProgressionService.cs:520-552) — and is DEAD, because the service component exists only on an unreferenced prefab under `MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)`; the arcade cards therefore fail open (nothing locks) while Menu_Main's QuestTrackView fails closed (a permanently locked chain at slider 0), and the FTUE tutorial system beside it is dead by the same test. So "there is nothing for a hardcore player to climb" is true of the build and false of the repository — the ladder needs instantiating, not designing. (3) The vessel unlock economy gates nothing about competitive access: no unlock check exists in the launch or spawn path (GameDataSO clamps to the mode's authored hull regardless of `isLocked`), so a day-one player can fly the locked Dolphin, Sparrow and Rhino in their own modes — which resolves the auditor's open question and demotes that capability from "live gate" to "picker filter in five multi-hull cards". Two smaller but load-bearing fixes: the Dolphin ships on the SCALAR flight model (`Dolphin.prefab:2178 vectorFlightModel: 0`), so its documented momentum-lock drift is unreachable code and SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md §6 is wrong — only Squirrel and Scarab ship the vector model; and the fleet is owner-authoritative on ALL vessels, not just the four carrying ClientNetworkTransform, which strengthens rather than softens the no-anti-cheat finding. Net: the dimension's verdict stands — deep vessels, thin and partly unreachable competitive scaffolding — but the fix list changes shape, from "build a progression and time-attack loop" to "wire up the one that already exists, author five comeback rates, and stop shipping a dead quest track and a dead tutorial on the main menu."
- CORRECTION ElementalComebackSystem (rubber-band): live — severity 'critical': "one joust behind puts the trailing team at level 10 in every element and switches on all four level-5 upgrades" -> live — but the magnitude is overstated ~10x. bonusLevels = deficit × rate is ONE element level per unit of deficit, not ten. The system is still mandatory, un-toggleable, and un-authored on 5 cards; the saturating modes are the crystal/prism-COUNT modes, not the small-target race modes the auditor named.: The finding survives — a mandatory rubber-band with an unauthored default is a real integrity problem — but the worked example is wrong. Rewrite it as: the default is inert-to-mild where the target is a small integer (Joust ≤+3 levels, AstroLeague/BroodRush goal counts), and saturating where the metric counts in the tens (Skim Race, Scurry: ≥10 crystals behind = every element pinned at level 10 and all four level-5 upgrades on). That inversion matters for the fix: the modes that need the rate authored DOWN are the count-metric ones, and Joust arguably needs it authored UP if a comeback is wanted at all. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/ElementalComebackSystem.cs:332-334 — `float bonusLevels = scoreDiff * rate; float normalizedBonus = Mathf.Max(0f, bonusLevels / 10f); ... rs.SetComebackModifier(element, normalizedBonus)`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/ResourceSystem.cs:281 SetComebackModifier takes NORMALIZED units; :137-139 MinElementalLevel -0.5 / MaxElementalLevel 1.5 / LevelScale 10; :197 SustainedCeiling 1.0; :251 GetLevel = floor(effective × 10) — so normalizedBonus 0.1 == +1 integer level; Assets/Resources/EndConditionOverrides.asset:17 joustCount 3 — the largest deficit Joust can ever show is 3, i.e. +3 levels max at rate 1.0, which does not by itself reach the level-5 unlock from a level-0 base; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/ElementalComebackSystem.cs:135-137 DefaultSourceFor: SkimRace/Scurry → CrystalsCollected — a 10-crystal deficit there DOES pin the layer at the ceiling (level 10), which is the real saturating case; Confirmed as claimed: SO_ArcadeGame.cs:48 default 1f; ArcadeGameSkimRace/Joust/Scurry/AstroLeague/BroodRush .asset all omit ComebackRatePerScoreDeficit; GameDataSO.cs:379 copies it; ElementalComebackSystem.cs:317 rate<=0 is the only opt-out and no lobby/player toggle exists)
- CORRECTION Vessel unlock economy as a gate on competitive access: live -> partial — no unlock check exists anywhere in the launch or spawn path; a locked hull is fully playable in its own mode: This also answers the auditor's first open question definitively. Consequence for the dimension: progression does NOT gate competitive access — a day-one player can fly the Dolphin in Rampage/Bends, the Sparrow in Dog Fight/Salvo/Wildlife and the Rhino in Peel the Cage/Astro League. The economy only restricts the PICKER in the five multi-hull cards (Scurry 3, Astro League 2, Brood Rush 5, Maelstrom 3, MP Freestyle 5). (Assets/_Scripts/Utility/DataContainers/GameDataSO.cs:387-390 + 457-470 — SyncFromArcadeGame publishes every authored vessel into AllowedVesselClasses (locked ones included) and ClampSelectedVesselToGame forces the mode's first authored hull; no IsLocked test; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Modals/ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:2243-2262 OnStartGameClicked — the only guards are the ready latch and the weekly-challenge attempt; nothing tests a selected ship; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Modals/ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:1334 BuildAvailableShips filters `!s.IsLocked`, and :1349-1352 InitializeDefaultShipFromAvailable sets the selection to null when the list is empty — so a single-hull locked mode shows an EMPTY picker and still launches with that hull; grep IsLocked across Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/*.cs, VesselSpawner.cs, VesselPrefabContainer.cs: no vessel-lock check (MultiplayerSetup.cs:368/382 IsLocked is UGS session locking); Assets/_SO_Assets/Classes/SO_Class_Scarab.asset:50-51 isLocked 0 / UnlockCost 100 with NO ownedFromStart — two hulls ship unlocked, not one; and Assets/_Scripts/System/CloudData/UGSDataService.cs:305-320 SyncHangarToVessels only ever calls vessel.Unlock(), never Lock(), so an editor-persisted debug unlock (SO_Vessel.cs:69-77 warns the editor writes Unlock() back into the asset) can never be re-locked by the cloud)
- CORRECTION Dolphin skim economy, drift-bought boost, Echo Sight (and the Squirrel entry's fleet-model evidence line): partial — cited as running the vector flight model with DriftThrottlePolicy.Locked ('momentum-lock drift', 'Squirrel/Scarab/Dolphin vector') -> the vector-model half is DEAD on the Dolphin — it ships on the scalar model and its Locked drift policy is unreachable code: The Dolphin's advertised signature — 'the drift freezes the velocity vector outright, so entering a drift at speed costs nothing' — is not what a player gets today; it flies the legacy scalar model where throttle pushes along Course, i.e. the exact defect SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md was written to fix. Combined with QA-DOLPHIN-SKIM ('nobody has ever seen a Dolphin skim work'), the Dolphin is the weakest-evidenced of the four 'deep' hulls, not the third-strongest. (Assets/_Prefabs/Spacevessels/Dolphin.prefab:2178 `vectorFlightModel: 0` (and :2180 `driftThrottlePolicy: 1`); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/VesselTransformer.cs:879-880 `if (vectorFlightModel) MoveShipVector(); else MoveShipScalar();`; :918 ComputeNoseAcceleration is called ONLY from MoveShipVector; :771-773 the Locked policy lives inside ComputeNoseAcceleration; Assets/_Prefabs/Spacevessels/Squirrel.prefab:909 = 1, Scarab.prefab:411 = 1, Sparrow.prefab:1191 = 0 — only two vessels ship the vector model; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActions/SQUIRREL_DRIFT.md §6 fleet table asserts 'Dolphin | vector | Locked'. Doc and code disagree; code wins.)
- CORRECTION Legacy PORT leaderboards screen (per-mode high scores): dead — evidence read as disabledScreens = [PORT] -> dead (confirmed) — but the serialized list is [ARK, PORT]: two menu screens are switched off, not one: Minor factual fix, but it widens the picture: the menu ships with two of its five screens switched off, so the surface a player can actually navigate is smaller than the screen inventory suggests. (Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:95581 `disabledScreens: 0100000003000000` — two little-endian int32s, 1 and 3; Assets/_Scripts/UI/ScreenSwitcher.cs:112 default `new() { MenuScreens.PORT, MenuScreens.ARK }`; :618-626 IsScreenDisabled / IsIndexDisabled gate both button and controller navigation; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Screens/LeaderboardsMenu.cs:64-70 FetchLeaderboard → PlayFab LeaderboardManager (confirmed inert data source))
- CORRECTION Vessel-locked mode list (used as evidence under the unlock-economy capability): Astro League listed as Rhino-only (from CLAUDE.md) -> Astro League authors TWO hulls — Rhino and Scarab: CLAUDE.md's 'Rhino-only' is stale for Astro League. It matters here because Scarab is one of the two hulls that ship unlocked, so Astro League is reachable with a real hull on a fresh account while Peel the Cage's picker is empty. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameAstroLeague.asset Vessels: guid ec97e344adb08f847a8f7649ab79088e (SO_Class_Rhino) + b136d82d275e0f8ea1feef29f0d416a4 (SO_Class_Scarab); Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameScurry.asset lists three (Sparrow, Manta, Squirrel); ArcadeGameBroodRush.asset lists five (Squirrel, Manta, Dolphin, Rhino, Serpent); ArcadeGameMaelstrom.asset lists three (Sparrow, Manta, Squirrel); Vessel guid map read from Assets/_SO_Assets/Classes/SO_Class_*.asset.meta)
- CORRECTION Ghosts / time trials / personal-best pacing: absent -> absent in the reachable build — but a time-attack ladder is fully authored and coded, and is dead only because its service is never instantiated: 'The Trackmania loop is not offered' is true of the build and false of the repository. The distinction changes the roadmap cost from 'design and build a time-attack mode' to 'instantiate one service and surface a number that is already persisted'. (Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuest_SkimRace.asset — IntensityUnlockStatType 1, Intensity3StatTarget 215, Intensity4StatTarget 270, 'Complete Hex Race Intensity 2 under 215 seconds'; Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs:530-552 RecordIntensityPlay evaluates that stat target to unlock intensity 3/4; Assets/_Scripts/UI/UGSStatsManager.cs:98-112 GetEvaluatedHighScore (per mode+intensity, golf-aware) exists with ZERO callers project-wide)
- ADDITION [dead] Game-mode quest progression chain + stat-gated intensity unlocks: The single biggest miss in the audit, and it cuts both ways. HARDCORE: this is a real designed mastery ladder — intensity 4 (the hardest AI and densest arena) earned by beating a TIME on intensity 3 — and it is unreachable, so the auditor's 'nothing to climb' verdict is right about the build and wrong about the design; standing the service up is a one-object fix, not a feature. CASUAL: with the service absent, the QuestTrackView in Menu_Main (guid 1566c719842df27479dce3e1fc98d76f, present in Menu_Main.unity) renders every quest after the first as LOCKED forever (QuestTrackView.cs:178-181) with its progress slider pinned at 0 (:373-380) — a permanently dead progression track on the main menu, which is worse for a new player than having none. Note the two fallbacks disagree by design accident: cards fail OPEN, the quest track fails CLOSED. (Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs:20 static Instance; :120-140 IsGameModeUnlocked; :360-416 GetMaxUnlockedIntensity / IsIntensityUnlocked / GetPlaysRequiredForIntensity; :520-552 RecordIntensityPlay unlocks intensity 3 then 4 by play count OR by a stat target; Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuestList.asset — 6 authored quests (SkimRace 'HEX RACE', Joust, Scurry, WildlifeBlitz, PartyGame, +1); Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuest_SkimRace.asset — PlaysToUnlockIntensity3 3, Intensity3StatTarget 215 s, Intensity4StatTarget 270 s; THE COMPONENT IS NEVER INSTANTIATED: `grep -rl 541692fb0a8f1b6478f85df5b78951a7 Assets/` (its script guid) returns exactly one file — `Assets/_Prefabs/MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/PlayerDataService.prefab` — and that prefab's own guid 5b7710d8d41620641ac818f27c001f16 is referenced by no scene or prefab. No AddComponent<GameModeProgressionService> exists; AppManager.cs never mentions Progression; Bootstrap.unity and Menu_Main.unity contain no 'Progression' object.; Fallbacks therefore all resolve open: ArcadeExploreView.cs:168 `bool isLocked = progressionService != null && !IsGameModeUnlocked(...)` → nothing locks; ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:1284-1287 the intensity lock is skipped → all four intensities selectable)
- ADDITION [dead] FTUE / tutorial / onboarding: No in-game teaching exists for any of the deep mechanics. HARDCORE: the skill ceiling the product is sold on — Squirrel analog drift depth, Rhino's hold-both-triggers energize ritual, Sparrow trigger discipline (pulse to keep the 1.5° cone), Dolphin bank-then-spend — is entirely undocumented in-game and undiscoverable; the ability lockup's control chip tells you WHICH button, never what the mechanic rewards. CASUAL: 'quick to fun' has no on-ramp at all; a first session is a player alone in the lava lamp with an autopilot vessel. This is the largest single gap between the documented product and the reachable one, alongside the progression chain above. (Assets/FTUE/Scripts — 26 C# files (TutorialFlowController, TutorialExecutorAdapter, FTUEEventManager, IntroWelcomeHandler, FreestylePromptHandler, LockModesExceptFreestyleHandler, OpenArcadeMenuHandler, TutorialUIView …); Per-script guid sweep over Assets/_Scenes + Assets/_Prefabs: TutorialFlowController 0 refs, FTUEEventManager 0 refs, every adapter and handler 0 refs. TutorialUIView is referenced only by Assets/FTUE/Prefabs/Captain_VO_FTUE.prefab, whose own guid 31a8c732468d1a14f817673f642b0cce is referenced by no scene or prefab.; CLAUDE.md documents the FTUE adapter architecture as a live system)
- ADDITION [absent] Host migration / rejoin-in-progress: HARDCORE: a match cannot survive the host leaving, and the host is a player — so a losing host rage-quitting destroys the result for everyone, with no reconnect and no partial credit. Combined with owner-authoritative movement and client-reported hits, this is the third leg of 'no competitive integrity guarantees'. CASUAL: mostly invisible in a friends party, and the bounce-to-menu recovery is graceful. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:481-495 — on host loss the client logs 'Host left/disconnected - bouncing to solo menu' and calls PartyInviteController.HandleHostLossAsync, re-establishing its own solo host in Menu_Main; grep -rn 'host migration|HostMigration' Assets/_Scripts: no implementation; Docs/STEAM_EA_INVESTOR_CHECKPOINT.html: 'No Steamworks networking, no host migration for Early Access'; 'Host migration and rejoin-in-progress — Disconnect handling exists. Migration was a multi-week task with Early-Access-acceptable absence.')
- ADDITION [live] Owner-authoritative movement is fleet-wide, not just the four ClientNetworkTransform vessels: Strengthens the auditor's client-trust finding rather than contradicting it: there is no server-authoritative vessel anywhere in the fleet, so a position/speed cheat is unblockable on every hull, not just the four the auditor found. HARDCORE: no anti-cheat surface exists and none is cheap to add. CASUAL: irrelevant in a friends party — and owner authority is what keeps flight feeling crisp on a Relay connection. (Assets/_Prefabs/Spacevessels/{Sparrow,Squirrel,Urchin,Scarab}.prefab carry the custom ClientNetworkTransform (guid fb01b7a2fa864b1468b5a2797ade4272), which Assets/_Scripts/Utility/Network/ClientNetworkTransform.cs:11-20 documents as 'putting trust on your clients'; Assets/_Prefabs/Spacevessels/{Dolphin,Rhino,Serpent,Manta}.prefab carry the STOCK Netcode NetworkTransform (guid da52bf8bbc1de48cfb221a6ff30f7972) with `AuthorityMode: 1` (Owner) — e.g. Dolphin.prefab:2104)
- ADDITION [absent] In-game network quality readout (ping / jitter / packet loss): HARDCORE: a player who loses a joust or a gunfight has no way to tell a skill loss from a latency loss, and no way to see that the host has an advantage. In a Relay-hosted, owner-authoritative game with client-reported hits, that is the single readout competitive players ask for first. CASUAL: no impact. Cheap to close — NetworkMonitor already polls the data. (CLAUDE.md lists `NetworkStatsManager` under Multiplayer key files; `find Assets -iname '*NetworkStats*'` returns nothing and `grep -rn NetworkStatsManager Assets/_Scripts` returns nothing — the class does not exist; The only in-build overlay is Assets/_Scripts/Utility/PerformanceBenchmark/DiagnosticsHUD.cs:36 (frame/perf rows), which owns roughly the first 44 screen px per Docs/GAME_MODE_TOPBAR.md; Docs/NetworkDiagnostics/ARCHITECTURE.md describes a NetDiag overlay built on NetworkMonitor; no UI script under Assets/_Scripts/UI references NetworkMonitor)
- ADDITION [absent] Aim / turn sensitivity and control rebinding: HARDCORE: on a mouse-flown one-thumb hull (Sparrow, Serpent, Grizzly, Scarab) there is no sensitivity slider at all, which is table stakes for anyone who aims for a living; MouseVirtualStick's unitsPerPixel is a hardcoded tuning value. The checkpoint's own mitigation is self-cancelling — Steam Input is named as the remapping answer on a launch that ships no Steam SDK. CASUAL: defaults are fine and the two invert toggles cover the common complaint (though Docs note Invert Y is inert on every one-thumb hull). (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Settings/GameSetting.cs:113-121 — the entire settings surface is MusicEnabled/SFXEnabled/HapticsEnabled/InvertYEnabled/InvertThrottleEnabled/JoystickVisualsEnabled + three level floats. No sensitivity, no dead zone, no response curve.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Settings/DisplayGraphicsSettings.cs:191-195 SetFieldOfView is the only flight-relevant tunable; grep -rn 'PerformInteractiveRebinding|RebindingOperation' Assets/_Scripts: no matches — the Input System's rebinding API is unused; Docs/STEAM_EA_INVESTOR_CHECKPOINT.html: 'Full rebinding UI — Invert-Y and invert-throttle shipped. Steam Input covers controller remapping with no work from us' — against 'Steam SDK: None at launch' on the same page)
- ADDITION [live] Uniform zero starting element levels across the fleet (clean-slate integrity): A genuine competitive-integrity strength the auditor asserted but did not verify at the asset level: every pilot starts every match at element level 0 on every hull, there is no captain/loadout/perk power layer, and nothing purchased or ground can be brought into a match. Worth stating positively for the market analysis — it is the one axis on which this beats most of its comparables. HARDCORE: outcomes are decided inside the match. CASUAL: a friend who just installed is not out-statted, only out-skilled. (All ten Assets/_SO_Assets/Classes/SO_Class_*.asset author `InitialResourceLevels: Mass 0, Charge 0, Space 0, Time 0`; Assets/_SO_Assets/ComebackProfiles/{AstroLeague,SkimRace}ComebackProfile.asset author InitialMass/Charge/Space/Time all 0 — the profile's only remaining job (per-vessel initial seeding) is authored to nothing; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/ResourceSystem.cs:170-185 InitializeElementLevels is the only seeding path; SO_Captain.InitialResourceLevels (SO_Captain.cs:25) reaches only the legacy Arcade.cs:62 / ArcadeExploreView.cs:316 paths; Assets/_Scripts/System/LoadOut/Loadout.cs — a saved loadout is intensity + player count + vessel + mode; there is no build, no perk, no equipment)
- ADDITION [live] Astro League mercy rule + golden-goal overtime: The auditor named golden goal but missed the mercy rule; together they make Astro League the ONLY mode in the game with real match-shape rules (early end on a blowout, overtime on a tie). Every other mode is one round, one turn, first-to-target. HARDCORE: the closest thing to a competitive ruleset the game has, and the template if series/overtime is ever wanted elsewhere. CASUAL: mercy caps blowouts, which is exactly the party-game instinct. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/AstroLeague/AstroLeagueController.cs:533-536 — `bool goldenGoal = phase == MatchPhase.Overtime; bool mercy = rule.IsObjectiveReached(gameData, out _); if (goldenGoal || mercy)`; AstroLeagueController.cs:589 `if (IsTiedAcrossActiveDomains() && settings.goldenGoalOvertime)`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/AstroLeague/AstroLeagueSettingsSO.cs:19 'Domain goal sum that ends the match early (mercy rule)'; :23 goldenGoalOvertime = true)
- ADDITION [dead] Curated TrainingGames / LeaderboardGames mode lists: Two curated lists that name exactly the two surfaces this dimension is missing — a practice/training set and a leaderboard-eligible set — sitting as data with no consumer. Reinforces that 'no practice mode, one leaderboard' is an unfinished wiring state rather than a design position. Also flags a live latent bug: the modal's fallback game list is the legacy roster, so any path that falls back to `gameList` lookup by mode (ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:603-612) fails for all 13 shipping cards. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/GameLists/TrainingGames.asset (5 modes) and LeaderboardGames.asset (5 modes) exist as authored SO_GameList assets; Neither asset's guid (7f397ad26f242934488f2c6e80adfeb5, e3b22fdbb4ee8d94a88d34ff1a9f2f15) is referenced by any scene or prefab; The DI-registered list is OrganicRematchGames.asset (guid 25a98002ea18f5d45b5e8b2098a06c57, wired on Assets/_Prefabs/CORE/AppManager.prefab:61) — SkimRace, Joust, Scurry, Maelstrom, AstroLeague, BroodRush, Rampage, PeelTheCage, WildlifeLiberation, DogFight, Bends, ScarabScramble, Salvo; AllGames.asset (referenced in Menu_Main only as ArcadeGameConfigureModal.gameList, a by-mode fallback lookup) contains ONLY legacy single-player cards — Elimination, DolphinDarts, BlockBandit, CosmicDrift, ShootingGallery, DuelForTheCell, Denial, CatNMouse, SlipNStride ×2, Rampage, MP Freestyle, OnlineDuelForTheCell — so that fallback can never resolve a modern card)

### Verdict
The audit is broadly reliable on structure and unusually well-sourced: the 13-card reachable roster, the 20 dead SO_ArcadeGame assets, the Maelstrom's 7-mode pool and race-to-6, the four-complete/four-half/four-shell fleet, the dead quest-chain progression, the dead Codex, the missing Salvo preview, the 200/50/0 crystal economy and the three never-opened modes all hold under direct file inspection. Three corrections matter most. First, the roster is less vessel-locked than claimed — nine of twelve are single-hull, not eleven, because Astro League offers Rhino+Scarab and Brood Rush offers six hulls alongside Scurry's three; the auditor's own entries contradict its summary here. Second, the freestyle hub is materially richer than reported: the Cell Selector authors no list and falls through to Cell.AvailableConfigs, which Menu_Main populates with ten cell configs, not seven. Third, several "dead" verdicts are actually "live and broken," which is worse for a paid Early Access build: the Arcade manager singleton exists in no scene, so Arcade.Instance is permanently null while two Hangar buttons still open a Training modal whose Launch dereferences it; and QuestTrackView ships active in Menu_Main with no service behind it, rendering a permanently frozen 0/6 quest ladder with an inert Claim button — the guided on-ramp the investor checkpoint promises appears to the player as a locked game. Smaller fixes: Co-op Wildlife Blitz is not in Build Settings at all (five hidden scenes ship, not six); five cards allow a solo launch, not six; the Rhino has four bound actions across three input events on every device (its gap is the empty elemental map, not the bindings); DailyChallengeSystem.prefab is in Bootstrap; SO_ArcadeGame.GolfScoring only sorts the PORT leaderboard and never touches match scoring; and MaxPartySlots is 6 with an invite gate of 4. The largest omissions are two reachability facts above the whole roster: there is no public matchmaking of any kind (the session-query path is unreachable under the locked eager-Relay design, so a friendless buyer gets bots in every mode), and a Windows IL2CPP player has never been verified to reach the main menu at all.
- CORRECTION Vessel-locking of the reachable roster ("eleven of the twelve are vessel-locked"; "Scurry is the only reachable mode offering a vessel CHOICE"): live (11/12 vessel-locked, 1 with choice) -> live, but NINE of twelve are single-vessel and THREE offer a choice: The auditor's own capability entries contradict each other — they list Brood Rush as offering six hulls and Astro League as Rhino+Scarab, then assert Scurry is the only mode with a choice. Guid-mapped asset data: 9 locked, 3 with a picker. Also note ArcadeGameMaelstrom.asset itself carries a 3-vessel list (Sparrow/Manta/Squirrel) that is inert, since GameDataSO.SyncFromArcadeGame republishes the drawn round's card. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameScurry.asset Vessels: 3 entries → SO_Class_Sparrow (7b7053dd…), SO_Class_Manta (b0e6ec54…), SO_Class_Squirrel (6fbeef29…); Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameAstroLeague.asset Vessels: 2 entries → SO_Class_Rhino (ec97e344…), SO_Class_Scarab (b136d82d…); Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameBroodRush.asset Vessels: 6 entries → Squirrel, Manta, Dolphin, Rhino, Serpent, Sparrow; Single-vessel cards: SkimRace, Joust, Rampage, PeelTheCage, WildlifeLiberation, DogFight, Bends, ScarabScramble, Salvo (1 entry each))
- CORRECTION Built-but-hidden modes — "MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp … all enabled" in Build Settings: dead (6 scenes shipped in the player binary with no UI path) -> dead — but only FIVE ship in the binary; Co-op Wildlife Blitz is not in Build Settings at all: Co-op Wildlife Blitz is more dead than claimed: its card names a scene that is on disk but not in the build, so even if a card reached it the load would fail in a player. The 'six unreachable scenes shipping in the binary' quick-win should say five. (ProjectSettings/EditorBuildSettings.asset — full m_Scenes list contains MinigameWildlifeBlitz, MinigameDuelForTheCell, MinigameFreestyleMultiplayer_Gameplay, MinigameDuelForCellMultiplayer_Gameplay, ArcadeGameMultiplayer2v2CoOpVsAI; there is NO entry for MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp; Assets/_Scenes/Multiplayer Scenes/MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp.unity exists on disk; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameCoOpWildlifeBlitz.asset SceneName: MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp)
- CORRECTION Solo play — "six reachable cards allow 1 player": live (6 solo-capable cards) -> live, but FIVE cards allow a 1-player launch: Also worth flagging: WildlifeLiberation and ScarabScramble carry MinDomainsAllowed: 2 alongside MinPlayersAllowed: 1, and ArcadeGameConfigureModal.ComputeMaxDomainCount clamps DC to the player count — so a 1-player launch of either produces a one-domain 'domain race'. (MinPlayersAllowed: 1 — ArcadeGameSkimRace.asset, ArcadeGameScurry.asset, ArcadeGameRampage.asset, ArcadeGameWildlifeLiberation.asset, ArcadeGameScarabScramble.asset; MinPlayersAllowed: 2 — ArcadeGameJoust, ArcadeGameMaelstrom, ArcadeGameAstroLeague, ArcadeGameBroodRush, ArcadeGamePeelTheCage, ArcadeGameDogFight, ArcadeGameBends, ArcadeGameSalvo)
- CORRECTION Freestyle hub — "seven authored cells" / "seven unlocked toys and seven cells": live (7 toys, 7 cells) -> live — 7 toys is right, but the Cell Selector offers TEN worlds: Understates the freestyle content by three worlds. Lattice is the boot world (index 0) and Barren is the Wanderway's bare canvas, so eight are 'destination' worlds plus the boot cell and the bare canvas. (Assets/_SO_Assets/Toys/Toy_CellSelector.asset — `cells: []` (no authored list); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Toys/CellSelectorToy.cs:200 — `var source = authored is { Count: > 0 } ? authored : _offeringCell.AvailableConfigs;`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Environment/Cell.cs:1986 — `public IReadOnlyList<CellConfigDataSO> AvailableConfigs => CellConfigs;`; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:1600 — `CellConfigs.Array.size` value: 10; entries map to Lattice, Yggdra, Daedala, Orrery, Zephyr, Caldera, Geode, Ourobor, Hesperides, Barren cell configs)
- CORRECTION Half-finished vessels — "Rhino.prefab 1 binding": partial (Rhino has a single bound ability) -> partial — Rhino has FOUR bound actions across three input events on every input device: The undercount comes from reading only `_inputEventShipActions`. The Rhino's real gap is its ElementalAbilityMap (all four entries `Input: 0`, `AbilityLabel: (open design slot)` except Mass 'Trail Slabs', all `UpgradeLabel` blank) — it has playable abilities but no elemental upgrade path and no derivable control chips. The same correction applies to the Squirrel, whose `_inputEventShipActions` is literally `[]` (everything lives in `_touchActionOverrides`/`_gamepadActionOverrides`). (Assets/_Prefabs/Spacevessels/Rhino.prefab `_inputEventShipActions` — InputEvent 0 → RhinoRampBoostAction (cb9bc8a3…) + GrowTrailAction (0c9c1d52…); Same prefab `_gamepadActionOverrides` — InputEvent 1 → RhinoShieldSwipeRightAction (b92a3e13…), InputEvent 2 → RhinoShieldSwipeLeftAction (4ff8e060…); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/R_VesselActionHandler.cs:207-218 — InputDeviceType.Keyboard, .MouseKeyboard and .DualMouse all resolve to `_gamepadOverrideActions`, so a keyboard/mouse pilot gets the swipes too)

The match-scoring truth is the controller override. - ADDITION [dead] The `Arcade` manager singleton exists in NO scene or prefab — the single root cause for training games, faction missions and the legacy daily challenge, and it leaves a THROWING button in the live Hangar: The auditor called training/missions 'dead', which understates it: the Hangar's Training modal is REACHABLE from two buttons in the shipping menu, lists real training games, and its launch button dereferences a null singleton — a NullReferenceException in front of the player. Four of the six training games also point at scenes that do not exist (BlockBandit, DolphinDarts, MazeRun, SlipAndStride). One deletion or one prefab placement resolves the whole cluster. - ADDITION [partial] QuestTrackView ships LIVE in Menu_Main with no progression service behind it — a permanently frozen quest ladder: The auditor recorded the service as dead but not that its UI is on screen. A new player sees a six-step quest chain (Crystal Capture → Hex Race → Joust → Wildlife Blitz → Party Game → Vessel Hangar) with step 1 unlocked, steps 2-6 locked forever, a 0/6 progress slider and an inert Claim button — i.e. the guided on-ramp the Steam checkpoint promises renders as permanently broken rather than absent. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/QuestTrackView.cs guid 1566c719842df27479dce3e1fc98d76f is referenced by Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity (line 122403), on a GameObject with m_IsActive: 1; QuestTrackView.cs:178-194 GetCardState — with a null service, index 0 is Unlocked and every later quest returns QuestItemState.Locked; QuestTrackView.cs:374-377 GetSliderValue returns 0 when the service is null; QuestTrackView.cs:262 `GameModeProgressionService.Instance?.ClaimQuestAndUnlockNext(...)` — the Claim button silently does nothing; Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs guid 541692fb0a8f1b6478f85df5b78951a7 is referenced only by 'Assets/_Prefabs/MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/PlayerDataService.prefab', whose own guid 5b7710d8d41620641ac818f27c001f16 is referenced by nothing)
- ADDITION [dead] No public matchmaking — the only route to a human opponent is a presence-lobby party invite: There is no quick-join, no browser, no skill matching, and no code path that reaches the session query in normal play. A Steam buyer with no friends online gets AI-only sessions in every mode. This is a structural limit on the 'party game for pilots' positioning that the auditor did not state. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:294 ExecuteMultiplayerSetup, :296-304 early-returns whenever `gameData.ActiveSession != null`; Same file :319-332 — the QuerySessions → IsJoinableSessionInfo → TryJoinFirstAvailable → StartSessionAsHost path sits entirely behind that early return; CLAUDE.md locked design: 'EAGER per-user Relay — every player hosts their own Relay-backed party session on entering Menu_Main', so ActiveSession is non-null for every online player by construction; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:181 is the only caller)
- ADDITION [live] Every reachable mode is exactly ONE round and one turn — the Maelstrom is the only multi-round structure in the product: Confirms and hardens the auditor's session-shape claim with scene data. There is no best-of-3, no set play, no series inside any single mode; the only structure above one match is the Maelstrom's race-to-6. (`numberOfRounds: 1` in all twelve mode scenes: MinigameSkimRace, MinigameJoust_Gameplay, MinigameScurryMultiplayer_Gameplay, MinigameAstroLeague, MinigameBroodRush, MinigameRampage, MinigameBends, MinigameDogFight, MinigameScarabScramble, MinigameSalvo, MinigameWildlifeLiberation, MinigamePeelTheCage; Assets/_SO_Assets/Maelstrom/MaelstromData.asset WinTarget: 6, PointsByPlace 2/1/0, MaxGames: 7)
- ADDITION [partial] A Windows IL2CPP player has never been verified to reach the main menu — the reachability gate above the whole roster: The launch plan is Windows-only paid Steam EA. Every content claim in this dimension is conditional on a player build the team's own ledger says has not been confirmed to boot. The auditor noted 'only the compile gate has ever passed' but did not surface that the outstanding P0 is the player build itself. (Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:33-56 QA-BUILD-WINDOWS-PLAYER ⬜ — 'every defect in this chain was invisible in the Editor'; edit-mode tests killed the UnityLinker (IL1005 / nunit.framework), and once fixed 'the player crashed on every login inside PauseMenu.Prewarm'; Docs/QA/ARCHIVE.md — the only passed item ever is QA-BUILD-COMPILE (5144ad269, 2026-08-14), which is the compile gate, not a player build)
- ADDITION [live] The arcade grid has exactly twelve card slots — a thirteenth playable mode would silently not render: The roster is at capacity by coincidence. Adding a 14th entry to OrganicRematchGames (or surfacing the hidden single-player cards, as the auditor's quick-win suggests) drops a card off the grid with no error — the loop is bounded by the scene's child count, and cards sort favourites-first then alphabetically, so which mode vanishes depends on the player's favourites. (Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity contains exactly 12 instances of GameCard (guid dbaebc1ed836d1847b41976e206448f5); the Arcade Screen prefab (10 cards) is not instanced in Menu_Main; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ArcadeExploreView.cs:155 `for (var i = 0; i < GameCards.Count && i < GameList.Games.Count && i < sortedGames.Count; i++)`; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/GameLists/OrganicRematchGames.asset — 13 games; ArcadeExploreView.cs:143-145 filters Maelstrom out, leaving exactly 12)
- ADDITION [partial] The vessel ownership record and the Hangar UI disagree about which hulls exist: Three consequences the auditor half-saw. (1) The Scarab is outside the economy entirely — it can never be recorded owned in HANGAR_DATA and is absent from the Hangar grid, yet it is the only hull for Scarab Scramble and is offered by Astro League. (2) Grizzly and Termite are inside the ownership reconciliation at 4,000 crystals but are NOT in the Hangar list, so they cannot actually be bought — the auditor's 'they can surface in UI while being unplayable' overstates the exposure. (3) The Hangar's purchasable set is six hulls (Squirrel free), all of which a mode force-equips anyway (GameDataSO.ClampSelectedVesselToGame). (Assets/_Scenes/Bootstrap.unity wires UGSDataService.vesselList → guid 49c7c10ad2901c74589c28317a4eb17b = SO_Classlist_All (9 hulls: Dolphin, Manta, Rhino, Urchin, Grizzly, Squirrel, Serpent, Termite, Sparrow — no Scarab); Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity wires HangarScreen.ShipList → guid 17dcd58a38dad2a498afe518e6a0bb58 = SO_Classlist_Classes (7 hulls: Sparrow, Manta, Serpent, Rhino, Squirrel, Dolphin, Urchin); Assets/_Scripts/System/CloudData/UGSDataService.cs:293-320 SyncHangarToVessels iterates vesselList and seeds/reads HANGAR_DATA from it; Assets/_SO_Assets/Classes/SO_Class_Scarab.asset — isLocked: 0, UnlockCost: 100, ownedFromStart absent)
- ADDITION [live] The launch card seats ZERO AI by default — solo play is opt-in, not automatic: Refines the auditor's 'solo play is multiplayer with bots'. A first-time solo player who presses Start on Skim Race without touching the stepper races alone against nobody; getting opponents requires understanding Add AI or the player-count stepper. That is a real first-session friction point for the casual pitch. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Modals/ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:1225-1232 — 'the card opens with no AI placed (by design call, 2026-08-27) — the host seats every bot by hand through Add AI. Seats the card's MINIMUM still owes beyond the humans draw EMPTY and are topped up domain-balanced at launch'; ArcadeGameConfigureModal.cs:1233 `config.PlayerCount = Mathf.Max(game.MinPlayersAllowed, CurrentPartyHumanCount)`; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/ServerPlayerVesselInitializerWithAI.cs:461-465 — shouldSeekPlayers only for Joust and DogFight; skill = intensity × 0.25)

There is a second one: the PORT screen ranks the injected roster per mode and per vessel class. The legacy LeaderboardGames list the auditor cited as evidence of dead cards is entirely unreferenced, so those dead modes never appear.
