(Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md:1325-1329 QA-P2-SERPENT-SKIMMER ⬜ — 'Expected: it FAILS the audit (inactive VacuumSkimmer, no impactor/container) and does not skim'; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameBroodRush.asset Vessels includes SO_Class_Serpent (8a288448…); Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Toys/ToyVesselRoster.cs:24-28 Default roster includes VesselClassType.Serpent; Assets/Resources/ElementalAbilityMaps/Serpent.asset — three '(open design slot)' entries, one 'Boost Duration', all UpgradeLabel blank)

### Verdict
The audit is substantially reliable — its central finding survives adversarial checking and got stronger under it. I re-verified the load-bearing claim from a second direction: the GameModeProgressionService script guid appears in exactly one file in the whole project (the unreferenced `MIgration_Prefabs (DELETE LATER)/PlayerDataService.prefab`), and Bootstrap.unity carries PlayerDataService, UGSDataService and UGSStatsManager as scene components but not that one — so the component was orphaned when the prefab was superseded by scene objects, and every gate it feeds silently opens. The three most important corrections are: (1) **the Profile screen is reachable, and the quest track is on it and rendering** — Menu_Main's ScreenSwitcher.screens has FIVE entries including id 5 (PROFILE) and disabledScreens decodes to only {ARK, PORT}, so the auditor's "clamps to the disabled PORT screen" chain never happens; QuestTrackView is enabled on the active ProfileScreen with its quest list wired, which means a player sees a six-step progression track permanently frozen on step 1 with five locked steps behind it — visibly broken rather than invisibly absent, and worse for a paid launch; (2) **the weekly leaderboard is World-only** — `friendsTabEnabled: 0` is serialized in the scene and `regionalLeaderboards: []` in the catalog, so two of the three tabs are dimmed and unpressable, killing the auditor's "am I beating my friends" claim while the unbacked rank-reward tooltip IS wired and visible; and (3) **the crystal wallet is invisible** — `CrystalCurrencyDisplay` exists in no scene or prefab, `unlockButtonText` is null and the unlock button has no text child, so a player never sees their balance or a vessel's price until they press UNLOCK, which removes the "I am N wins away" meter that is the entire retention function of the currency. The auditor also missed four live-or-booted systems in this dimension: identity customization (name + avatar, cloud-persisted, reachable from five wired buttons), per-vessel mastery telemetry written to HANGAR_DATA and read by no UI, a CallToAction nudge singleton booted from Bootstrap whose data loader is entirely commented out, and the Maelstrom's cumulative intensity→mode-pool ladder. One numeric fix: nine of the twelve grid cards lock to a single hull, not ten. Everything else in the inventory checked out, including the payout table (uniform 200/50/0 even in the five scenes carrying the retired `winnerCrystalReward: 5` key), the single earn/single spend topology, the stubbed platform sign-in, and the absence of Steamworks, achievements, earned cosmetics and scheduled notifications.
- CORRECTION Quest track / claim UI (Profile screen): dead -> partial: The screen is reachable and the quest track renders — it is FROZEN, not absent, which is worse for the player than unreachable. With GameModeProgressionService.Instance null, QuestTrackView.GetCardState (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/QuestTrackView.cs:180-193) returns Unlocked for card 0 and Locked for cards 1-5 (the `service != null &&` guards), so no card ever reaches ReadyToClaim and the claim button never lights; ClaimQuestAndUnlockNext at :262 is a null-conditional no-op. A player who opens Profile sees a six-step progression track permanently stuck on step 1 with five locked steps behind it. The auditor's conclusion (no guided progression ships) is right; the mechanism is not. (Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:95566-95576 — ScreenSwitcher.screens is FIVE entries: id 4 (Hangar), 1 (Ark), 2 (Home), 3 (Port), **5 (Profile)**. The auditor read only the first four.; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:95581 `disabledScreens: 0100000003000000` decodes to two ints, 1 (ARK) and 3 (PORT) — PROFILE is NOT disabled.; ScreenSwitcher.GetIndexForScreen(PROFILE) therefore returns index 4 (found in the list), not the enum fallback 5; NavigateTo clamps to max = GetScreenCount()-1 = 4, which IS the Profile screen. The 'clamps to the disabled PORT screen' chain never happens.; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:150119-150137 — GameObject 'ProfileScreen' m_IsActive: 1; its RectTransform 5823808243158720794 is the screens[4] root.; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity:122394-122421 — QuestTrackView (guid 1566c719842df27479dce3e1fc98d76f) sits on that same ProfileScreen GameObject, m_Enabled: 1, with questList wired to GameModeQuestList.asset, questItemPrefab, slider, ghost slider and scroll rect all assigned.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/ScreenSwitcher.cs:815-818 OnClickProfileNav → NavigateTo(MenuScreens.PROFILE); Menu_Main's 'ProfileLink' nav button invokes it.)
- CORRECTION Weekly challenge leaderboard (World / Regional / Friends): partial -> partial — but World-only; the Friends tab is authored OFF: Status stays partial, but the auditor's casual_fit claim ('Friends tab re-ranks 1..n so "am I beating my friends" is answerable') is false as shipped: two of the three tabs are dimmed and unpressable, so the only ladder a player can see is the global World board. Turning it on is one serialized bool — worth knowing it is a flag, not missing code. The rank-reward tooltip IS wired (rankRewardButton/rankRewardPanel/rankRewardBackdrop all assigned in the scene), so the auditor's 'a promise with nothing behind it' gap is confirmed and visible. (Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity — WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardModal serialized block: `friendsTabEnabled: 0` (worldTab/regionalTab/friendsTab all wired, defaultScope: 0 = World).; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Modals/WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardModal.cs:71-73 — that flag 'keeps the Friends tab dimmed and unpressable even when the friends service [is] ready'; :41-43 a scope with nothing configured is dimmed and non-interactable rather than hidden.; Assets/Resources/WeeklyChallengeCatalog.asset:`regionalLeaderboards: []` — Regional has no board either.)
- ADDITION [live] Player identity customization (display name + avatar): The auditor's 'Cosmetics / skins / trails as rewards = absent' row is correct about EARNED cosmetics, but the summary and gaps ('nothing to customise or earn') overstate it: a player can pick a name and an avatar on day one, both roam via Cloud Save, and the avatar is what appears on the end-game score cards and the top-bar domain columns. Note the intended host widget for this (ArcadeProfileWidget.cs, AvatarButtonView.cs) is in NO scene and NO prefab — the live openers are hand-wired buttons — so the entry point is easy to lose in a UI pass. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ProfileIconSelectView.cs:23 — ModalWindowManager with an avatar grid (SO_ProfileIconList) and a display-name panel; on GO 'PlayerDataSelectModal' in Menu_Main (m_IsActive: 1, component fileID 8675064058193410727).; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity — five persistent UnityEvent calls target that component: `OpenDisplayName` at lines 8593, 29527, 58706 and `OpenAvatar` at lines 101586, 113819, plus ModalWindowIn/Out wiring at 76099/93185/150991. So both flows are reachable from real buttons.; Assets/_Scripts/ScriptableObjects/SO_ProfileIconList.cs:13-24 — ProfileIcon is {Name, Id, IconSprite}; there is NO unlock, price or gating field.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/PlayerProfileData.cs:33-47 — DisplayName + AvatarId live in ProfileIdentity, cloud key PLAYER_PROFILE, sole writer PlayerDataService.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ProfileScreen.cs:46-58 — the Profile screen displays the chosen name and avatar.)
- ADDITION [partial] Crystal wallet visibility (balance and price on screen): The economy works and the purchase completes, but a player cannot see how many crystals they have, or what a vessel costs, anywhere in the menu until they open a confirmation panel — and there is no wallet readout at all outside the Hangar. That removes the 'I am 3 wins away' feedback that is the entire retention function of a soft currency, and it makes the +200 badge on the end-game card (PlayerScoreCard.ShowCrystalReward) a number with no total behind it. Related doc-vs-code note: Docs/ECONOMY_AND_PRICING.md:273 warns 'Debug crystals ship in the build' — that is stale. Assets/_Scripts/Utility/Tools/LogControlWindow.cs:1 is `#if UNITY_EDITOR` around an EditorWindow and is in no scene, so minting cannot reach a player build. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/Hangar/CrystalCurrencyDisplay.cs:18 — the only component that subscribes to PlayerDataService.OnCrystalBalanceChanged for display; it appears in NO scene and NO prefab (grep of its guid across Assets/_Scenes and Assets/_Prefabs returns nothing).; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity — HangarVesselDetailView serialized block: `unlockButtonText: {fileID: 0}` (null), so HangarVesselDetailView.cs:187-204 writes 'UNLOCK - 4000' / 'UNLOCKED' to nothing.; Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity — the UnlockVesselButton's RectTransform 8257213038988619169 has `m_Children: []`: the button carries an Image and a Button and no text child at all.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/Hangar/HangarVesselGridCard.cs:61-65 — the grid card shows a lock overlay only; no price.; The balance and the price only ever render after the player presses unlock: HangarVesselDetailView.cs:224-233 activates unlockPanel/spendCrystalsPanel and fills crystalAmountText (wired: fileID 166624957435291541, GO 'MainDetailText') and spendCrystalsDetailText.)
- ADDITION [partial] Per-vessel mastery telemetry (HANGAR_DATA): The auditor's per-mode-bests row missed the vessel half of the same write. Also a refinement to that row's evidence: UGSStatsManager.GetEvaluatedHighScore (line 98) has ZERO callers anywhere in Assets/_Scripts — not 'readers only in dead PlayFab views'. So both halves of the stats layer are write-only: 'how much have I flown the Dolphin, and how good am I with it' is already recorded and cloud-persisted, and there is no screen that shows it. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/UGSStatsManager.cs:176-200 ReportVesselTelemetry — per-vessel GamesPlayed, LastUsedUtcMs, FlightTimeSeconds and best drift/boost/prisms-damaged, written into HANGAR_DATA alongside that vessel's unlock state.; Assets/_Scripts/System/CloudData/UGSDataService.cs:333-338 — HangarCloudData.RecomputePreferredVessel derives a 'most hours played' PreferredVessel.; Callers are the same four reporters as the mode stats: SkimRaceScoreTracker.cs:127, ScurryStatsReporter.cs:54, SinglePlayerWildlifeBlitzScoreTracker.cs:113, JoustStatsReporter.cs:58 — none of the nine newer modes.; Consumers: Assets/_Scripts/Utility/Tools/LogControlWindow.cs:1182 (editor window) and Assets/_Scripts/System/Instrumentation/AnalyticsServiceFacade.cs:476 (a 'preferred_vessel' analytics property). No runtime UI reads it.)
- ADDITION [dead] Call-to-action nudge system ("new thing here" badges): A booted singleton with a fully wired consumer surface and no data. This is the 'there is something new here' surface a launch would use to point players at the weekly challenge or a new mode, and it costs one authored list to turn on. (Assets/_Prefabs/CORE/CallToActionManager.prefab is instantiated in Assets/_Scenes/Bootstrap.unity (prefab guid c5bedb24f471a7b43ab346332d4ea40e).; Assets/_Scripts/System/CallToAction/CallToActionSystem.cs:82-89 LoadCallsToAction — the entire body is commented out under a `testMode` branch, so ActiveCallsToAction is always empty.; Assets/_Scripts/System/CallToAction/CallToActionSystem.cs:23-28 — a second commented-out block that would have populated ActiveTargets.; CallToActionTarget components are authored on the live game cards (Assets/_Prefabs/UI Elements/GameCard.prefab) and ArcadeExploreView.cs:177-180 sets each card's TargetID from SO_ArcadeGame.CallToActionTargetType — so the consumer side is fully wired to a producer that emits nothing.)

On a Windows-only Steam launch a local notification is the wrong tool anyway, but it means the weekly challenge's return prompt is 'the player remembers'. (Packages/manifest.json:22 — `com.unity.mobile.notifications: 2.4.2` is installed.; No first-party code uses it: grep for `Unity.Notifications`, `AndroidNotification`, `iOSNotification`, `ScheduleNotification` across Assets/_Scripts returns nothing.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Notification System/Payload/NotificationAPI.cs is an in-app SOAP toast channel, and `NotificationAPI.Notify` has ZERO callers anywhere in Assets/_Scripts.)
- ADDITION [dead] Arcade loadouts (per-game saved configurations): A saved per-mode configuration (vessel + intensity + player count) is a low-cost 'my setup' surface and it is switched off, not missing. The launch panel now asks these questions fresh every time. (Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ArcadeLoadoutView.cs:50-60,:108,:262-270 — the only consumer of LoadoutSystem.; In Menu_Main it sits on GO 'Loadout' with m_IsActive: 0, under Arcade_Panel(1) < ArcadeScreenModal(1).; Assets/_Scripts/System/LoadOut/{Loadout,ArcadeGameLoadout,LoadoutSystem}.cs are referenced by no scene and no prefab; nothing outside ArcadeLoadoutView calls LoadoutSystem.)
- ADDITION [partial] End-of-match reward ceremony (EndGameSequencer): The reward moment itself is live and reasonably choreographed; the two progression notifications it was built to carry are dead for the same reason the quest chain is. Worth listing separately because it is the surface any new reward (rank prize, participation payout, unlock) would plug into — it already exists. (Assets/_Scripts/Utility/DataContainers/EndGameSequencer.cs:14-30 — on OnWinnerCalculated it flies the local vessel through a random AI flourish, plays the GameEnd SFX, reveals a randomized win/lose line from EndGameMessageSetSO, holds, then raises OnShowGameEndScreen (the only path to the Scoreboard).; Lives on the EndGameStatsPanel object inside EndGameStatsPanel.prefab, nested by every game canvas, so it runs in every mode.; EndGameSequencer.cs:79-99 subscribes GameModeProgressionService.Instance.OnQuestCompleted and OnIntensityUnlocked — both null-guarded, and Instance is always null, so the two progression toasts never fire.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/PlayerScoreCard.cs:108 ShowCrystalReward — the '+N' badge is the one reward beat that does fire.)
- ADDITION [live] Maelstrom intensity ladder (the draw pool widens with intensity): The auditor covered Maelstrom's race-to-6 but not this: choosing a higher intensity does not merely make the same modes harder, it widens which modes can be drawn. It is the only 'the game opens up as you push' mechanic that actually ships — session-scoped, authored data, nothing persisted. (Assets/_SO_Assets/.../MaelstromData.asset — `IntensityTiers` is a cumulative ladder: intensity 1 adds Joust, Skim Race and Scurry; 2 adds Rampage and Peel the Cage; 3 adds The Bends; 4 adds Scarab Scramble. GameQueue lists all seven.; Docs/ArcadeLaunch/ARCHITECTURE.md describes the tier list as cumulative — a tier lists what it ADDS — with an empty ladder falling back to the legacy every-mode pool.)

### Verdict
The audit is substantially reliable on architecture and on absence — every claim about what does NOT exist (store assets, localisation, codex runtime UI, FTUE, accessibility consumers, Steamworks) checked out exactly, and the platform-law binding, palette wiring, goal stack, tails/jets, elemental bars and mode-preview inventories are all confirmed at the cited files. Its weakness is the opposite direction: three of the capabilities it rated 'live' are gated or broken in ways only asset inspection reveals. The three most important corrections are, first, that the arcade grid ships almost entirely LOCKED — `progressionConfig` is assigned in no scene or prefab, so GameModeProgressionService falls back to the code default `alwaysUnlockedModes = { Maelstrom }`, and Maelstrom is explicitly excluded from the grid, leaving a fresh player with one playable card (Crystal Capture, capped at intensity 2) beside eleven greyed ones, gated behind roughly six matches of quest work; the auditor listed this as an open question rather than answering it, and it is a one-field fix that currently withholds the exact breadth the product is sold on. Second, the end-game panel and the connecting panel are NOT live on the majority of shipped modes: GameCanvas-SkimRace.prefab carries eight dangling cross-prefab references into CORE/GameCanvas.prefab — including `connectingPanel`, the MiniGameHUD `view`, and the GameOverPanel's Continue button, Home button, best/high score texts and stats panel — and that fork is instanced by 12 of the 13 shipped modes' scenes, so the match-exit UI is driving objects that are not in the scene. Third, the audio picture is materially better than reported: 42 FMOD EventReferences are wired on the Bootstrap AudioSystem instance covering menu, rewards, pause, score reveal and the whole gameplay SFX layer, so 'would read as silence in a trailer' overstates a real but narrower problem (one music track for the whole game, boost muted by an FMOD loop-region bug, engine loops on 3 of 12 hulls). Smaller but load-bearing corrections: the Unity splash is already disabled with a custom Froglet logo, so that quick-win is spent; ModeControlsLibrary is not empty but carries 17 per-mode ability entries; and Rhino joins Dolphin and Urchin on `_Test` placeholder geometry, putting four of thirteen modes on test art. The largest genuine misses are the live five-rig main-menu camera over the running cell — the game's actual first frame and its best free trailer shot — the Astro League broadcast goal replay that already manufactures highlight footage in one mode, and the absent Windows application icon with a non-resizable 1024×768 default window. The auditor's central judgement stands and is if anything understated: the presentation engineering is strong and almost none of it has been rendered by Unity, with one compile check as the entire verification record.
- CORRECTION Arcade grid mode discoverability: live -> partial — 12 cards render, but 11 of them are LOCKED on a fresh profile because the progression config asset is not wired: The auditor verified the LIST (13 SO_ArcadeGame assets injected via AppManager.prefab:61 → OrganicRematchGames.asset, every SceneName on disk — that part is exactly right) but did not resolve its own open question about the lock state. A brand-new player's arcade screen is one playable card (Crystal Capture, intensity 1-2) beside eleven greyed, non-interactable ones. Even if `ProgressionConfig.asset` were wired it would be 7 of 12. This is the single biggest first-impression fact in the dimension and it is a one-field inspector fix, not a design problem. (Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs:44-54 — `Config` returns `progressionConfig` if set, else `ScriptableObject.CreateInstance<SO_ProgressionConfig>()` (the CODE defaults); `grep -rn "progressionConfig" Assets/_Scenes/ Assets/_Prefabs/` returns ZERO hits — the asset is never assigned, so the runtime default is always used; Assets/_Scripts/ScriptableObjects/SO_ProgressionConfig.cs:33 — code default `alwaysUnlockedModes = new() { GameModes.Maelstrom }` (ONE mode) and :47 `defaultMaxIntensity = 2`; Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/ProgressionConfig.asset:17-24 — the AUTHORED asset lists 7 always-unlocked modes (36,2,39,40,41,42,44). None of that reaches the game.; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ArcadeExploreView.cs:143-144 — Maelstrom is explicitly EXCLUDED from the grid, so the single default-unlocked mode is not even a card; Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs:127-139 — `IsGameModeUnlocked` = always-unlocked ∪ first quest ∪ saved unlocks; Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuestList.asset:16 quest[0] = GameModeQuest_Scurry.asset (`GameMode: 35`, DisplayName `CRYSTAL CAPTURE`); Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionData.cs:27 `UnlockedModes = new()` — empty on a fresh profile; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/GameCard.cs:262-281 `SetLocked` — lock overlay on, background tinted, `btn.interactable = false`)
- CORRECTION End-game presentation (score reveal, VICTORY banner, scoreboard) and the connecting panel: live -> partial — broken on the GameCanvas-SkimRace fork, which 11 of 17 gameplay scenes use: The auditor cited UI_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT §3.1's "connectingPanel null in both prefabs, overridden only in SkimRace (unverified)" and read the override as evidence it works. The override points into another prefab ASSET. So on the fork the connecting panel reference, the MiniGameHUDView `view` reference, and five GameOverPanel fields including the Continue and Home buttons resolve to asset objects that are not in the scene — the exit path from a finished match. This is the same defect class in the two surfaces the auditor rated most confidently live, on the majority of shipped modes. - CORRECTION Audio coverage (FMOD): partial, with the gap rated major: "would read as silence in a trailer" -> partial — correct status, but materially better wired than described: 42 FMOD EventReferences are live on the Bootstrap AudioSystem: Status unchanged, but the framing needs correcting for a market analysis: the menu and the per-event gameplay layer are broadly wired, not silent. The real content gaps are narrower and more specific — ONE music track for menu + freestyle + 13 modes, boost muted by an FMOD authoring bug, engine loops on 3 of 12 hulls, and the unrouted VCAs. "Would read as silence" is true only of the music bed and the engines. (Assets/_Scenes/Bootstrap.unity — the AudioSystem prefab instance (script guid 041326fa7f1db0944a3fa50cbacd061c, component fileID 5246419679807688196) carries EventReference overrides for 42 distinct fields: musicEvent, optionClick/openView/closeView/switchView/switchScreen, small/bigReward, upgrade, confirmed, denied, redeemTicket, letsGo, pauseOpen/pauseClose, scoreReveal, gameEnd, blockDestroy, boostActivate, crystalCollect, crystalSkim, creatureDeath, explosion, floraCollision, gunFire, mineExplode, projectileLaunch, shieldActivate/Deactivate, speedBurst, energyGain, driftStart, trackImpact, vesselImpact, joustScored/joustReceived + 4 joust element buffs, 4 comeback element events, 4 element-received events; Assets/_Scenes/Bootstrap.unity:3452 `value: event:/Music/Music`; :3462 `event:/SFX/Oneshots/Gameplay sfx/Boost Activate`; :3247 Gun Fire; :3282 Explosion; :3557 Crystal Collect; `Cosmic Shore/Metadata/Event/*.xml` = 67 events, exactly one named `Music` (confirmed by enumerating every event's name property); Docs/AudioSystem/CHARLES_TASKS.md:19-25 — C2: Boost Activate has a loop region so the code refuses to fire it, "boost is silent and logs one error per session"; ShipAudioController guid 86d0449d8c3c8498592cf0c29f6fd9e7 appears in exactly 3 of 12 vessel prefabs (Sparrow, Squirrel, Rhino) — confirmed; Cosmic Shore/Build/Desktop/{Master,Master.strings,SFX}.bank exist — answering the auditor's open question about bank placement (they are in the FMOD project's Desktop build folder, not Assets/StreamingAssets))

- ADDITION [live] Live main-menu world with a rotating five-rig cinematic camera (the lava lamp): The auditor covered world art and the arcade preview window but never listed the main menu itself, which is the game's actual first frame and its best free marketing surface: an AI-piloted vessel laying conserved-mass prism trails inside a living cell, framed by five authored camera rigs that cross-fade every 45 seconds over a 3-second glide. It is also the freestyle playfield — one tap and the same shot becomes the game. Hardcore: nothing to master, but it advertises the simulation honestly (the ecology is really running behind the UI). Casual: the strongest zero-friction hook in the build — a player who never presses a button has already seen the product, and it is the shot a 30-second trailer should open on. - ADDITION [live] Astro League goal replay — broadcast-framed instant replay on a dedicated replay camera: A genuine sports-broadcast presentation feature, shipped and on by default, that the auditor missed entirely — it fills the goal-reset window with a slow-motion ghost replay rather than a dead pause, and it is the one system in the build that manufactures highlight footage automatically. Hardcore: lets a player read the carom that scored, which is the skill Astro League is about. Casual: the 'ooh' beat a party game needs, and it costs the viewer nothing to understand. Marketing note: this plus the sphere court's BANK x{n} toast is the readiest trailer moment in the repo, and it exists in exactly one mode — the pattern is worth generalising to Scarab Scramble's hoops before capture week. - ADDITION [absent] Windows/Standalone application icon and window presentation: For a paid Windows Steam release the desktop/taskbar icon and the shortcut art come from the Standalone icon set, and the only icon configured is an Android adaptive-icon PNG standing in as the generic default. `resizableWindow: 0` means a windowed player cannot be resized or dragged to a second monitor — a first-minute friction point on PC that the mobile-first origin explains and does not excuse. The 1024×768 default is a 4:3 mobile-era value. All four are inspector fields, minutes of work, and all four are visible before the game even draws a frame. Hardcore: window/monitor control is table stakes for a PC audience. Casual: the icon is the product's face in the library and on the taskbar. (ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:305-312 — the only `m_BuildTargetIcons` entry has a blank `m_BuildTarget:` with one 128×128 icon, guid 0e99d9f594c253847babdeb6d4469358 = Assets/_Graphics/Icons/App Icons/Android Adaptive Icon/CSThumbApp.png; No Standalone/Windows entry under `m_BuildTargetPlatformIcons` (Android, iPhone, tvOS, WebGL blocks are present); ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:102 `resizableWindow: 0`; ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:48-49 `defaultScreenWidth: 1024`, `defaultScreenHeight: 768` (4:3); ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:150 `bundleVersion: 0.2.0`; :15-16 companyName `Froglet Games`, productName `Cosmic Shore`)
- ADDITION [partial] Quest progression gating of first-session content (the misconfigured lock chain): Distinct from the arcade-grid correction: this is the capability that decides what the first hour looks like, and it is authored but unreachable. The chain demands the player reach intensity 4 in Crystal Capture — which itself requires 3 plays to unlock intensity 3 and 3 more for intensity 4 — before a second mode appears. A player who paid $15 for 'the party game for pilots' with ~16 minigames is gated to one mode for roughly six matches. Hardcore: a mastery ladder is defensible in principle, but this one gates BREADTH rather than depth, which is the opposite of what the pitch promises. Casual: fatal — the thing that sells the game (many short modes with friends) is the thing withheld. Two candidate fixes are both one-line: wire ProgressionConfig.asset (7 always-unlocked modes), or set `alwaysUnlockedModes` to the full grid for Early Access. (Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuestList.asset:16-21 — a 6-quest chain: Scurry → SkimRace → Joust → WildlifeBlitz → PartyGame → VesselHangarUnlock; Assets/_SO_Assets/GameModeQuest/GameModeQuest_Scurry.asset — `TargetType: 6, TargetValue: 4`, "Unlock Intensity 4 to unlock next game mode", with `PlaysToUnlockIntensity3: 3` and `PlaysToUnlockIntensity4: 3`; Assets/_Scripts/System/Progression/GameModeProgressionService.cs:44-54 (config fallback), :127-139 (unlock test), :76,112 (`EnsureFirstModeUnlocked`); `grep -rn "progressionConfig" Assets/_Scenes/ Assets/_Prefabs/` → no hits (the authored ProgressionConfig.asset is orphaned); Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/QuestTrackView.cs:181,380 — quest track UI reads the same service; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/QuestItemCard.cs:112 — 'Coming Soon' placeholder copy; Docs/STEAM_EA_INVESTOR_CHECKPOINT.html — C9 quest dialogue authoring not started; "Quest and progression system tested end to end and finalised" is a Week 5 milestone)
- ADDITION [partial] Weekly challenge card and leaderboard panel in the menu: A live retention/marketing surface sitting at the top of the arcade screen that the auditor did not inventory. Presentation-wise it is the only place in the menu that says 'come back on Tuesday', and the only competitive surface with a global ranking. Partial rather than live for two reasons visible in code and docs: the ranking depends on three human-set UGS dashboard fields, one of which (ascending sort on a time score) renders a plausible upside-down board if wrong; and no avatar travels with a leaderboard entry, so rows keep the template art. Hardcore: a weekly time-attack with a global board is real competitive integrity and cheap to market ('this week's run'). Casual: one fixed ask, one attempt a day, no lobby to configure — the lowest-friction competitive entry in the build. (WeeklyChallengeCard (guid 86bc0f50b85e2d943ab5dbcf8db7b80b) is present in Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity; WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardPanel (guid 03ee08d6b2a445d69277abce7172dd54) is present in Assets/_Scenes/Menu_Main.unity; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Views/ArcadeExploreView.cs:110-112 — `WeeklyChallengeCard.Bind(this)` on every repopulate; :101-104 the challenge button gets its own D-pad nav row above the grid; Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/WeeklyChallengePlayButton.cs, Modals/WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardModal.cs, ScriptableObjects/WeeklyChallengeCatalogSO.cs all exist; Docs/WEEKLY_CHALLENGE.md — three UGS dashboard settings (ascending sort, keep-best, weekly reset with archiving) that no code can enforce; the sort order 'fails silently' with a correct-looking but inverted board)
- ADDITION [absent] Steam platform presentation layer (achievements, overlay, rich presence, Steam Input glyphs): Explicitly out of scope for EA per the brief, but it belongs in a presentation/marketability inventory because four visible things follow from it: no achievements (a Steam store page section stays empty and a major casual completion hook is absent), no overlay-verified screenshot/friends flow, no rich presence in a game whose party layer is its differentiator, and no Steam Input glyph query — which is exactly the data the ability lockup would need to fix the PS-shows-Xbox-letters defect the auditor noted. Hardcore: no achievement or ranked scaffolding to chase beyond the weekly board. Casual: 'what did my friend just unlock' is a discovery channel that will not exist at launch. (Packages/manifest.json contains no Steamworks or Facepunch entry; Assets/Plugins/ = Android, Demigiant, FMOD, NativeShare, Obvious, ParrelSync, iOS, tvOS — no Steamworks; `grep -rni "steamworks|steam_api" Assets/_Scripts --include=*.cs` returns only two forward-looking comments: Assets/_Scripts/System/Monetization/IEpisodeTokenPurchaseProvider.cs:20 and EpisodeTokenController.cs:78 ("once the Steamworks SDK and its verification backend land"); Docs/STEAM_EA_INVESTOR_CHECKPOINT.html cut list — "Steam Input covers controller remapping with no work from us"; Week 3 milestone "Build pipeline complete and overlay verified"; Assets/Resources/ControlGlyphSet.asset — one sprite per pad control shared across families (Docs/ABILITY_LOCKUP.md records the stated cost: a PlayStation player sees Xbox A/B on the Sparrow))
- ADDITION [planned] Style-guide typefaces are not installed (T5 blocks the type system): The auditor recorded 'six live font families' as a symptom but not the cause: the approved style foundation's type stack does not exist in the project, so every screen is necessarily still on the mobile-era Rajdhani/Liberation mix and no amount of layout work can land the intended look. This also blocks the tabular-numeric role the goal stack and score columns want (JetBrains Mono / `<mspace>` per face, UI_REDESIGN_TASKS.md:283-284). Hardcore: score and time readouts that do not use tabular figures visibly jitter digit-to-digit — a small but constant competitive-readability tax. Casual: typography is most of what makes a screenshot read as 'finished'. Cost is a font download plus TMP asset generation, i.e. hours, and it unblocks T6. (Docs/UI_REDESIGN_TASKS.md:19-20 — T5 'Download & install TMP fonts' TODO, T6 'TMP Style Sheet + Aldrich audit' TODO and blocked on T5; :24 "T6 needs T5's font assets to exist"; Docs/UI_REDESIGN_TASKS.md:257-267 — required: Chakra Petch 400/500/600/700 in Assets/_Graphics/Fonts/ChakraPetch/, Space Grotesk 300/400/500/600, JetBrains Mono 400/500/700, TMP assets at SDFAA sampling 90 padding 9 atlas 1024²; On disk: Assets/_Graphics/Fonts/ holds only Rajdhani (5 weights), LexendDeca-SemiBold, arial, LiberationSans Glowing. ALDRICH-REGULAR and ChakraPetch-Regular exist only as single weights inside Assets/Unity Assests/TextMesh Pro/Resources/Fonts & Materials/ (the TMP package folder), and Space Grotesk / JetBrains Mono are absent entirely; Docs/STYLE_FOUNDATION.md header — Version 0.3.2, reference 1920×1080 PPU 240)
- ADDITION [partial] Prism tier / palette rendering has never been seen in engine, and the corridor risk is a whole-arena failure: The auditor listed these as separate capability rows and separate gaps; stated as one capability it is the dimension's central risk. Every colour and readability claim in this audit — tiers, crystal collectability, the corridor dither, the vision band, the charge crystal — rests on hand-authored HLSL and generated palette values that no Unity render has confirmed, while the project's entire verification record is one compile check. The compound failure (a shader that does not compile) is not a degraded look, it is a magenta arena on first launch, which is simultaneously the P0 QA item and the thing a store screenshot would capture. Hardcore: unverified colour contract means tier legibility — food vs armour vs trap — is unproven at speed. Casual: a magenta first boot is a refund. The mitigation is an afternoon in the Editor and it gates capture week, not the other way round. (Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md — 65 distinct QA-* items, 59 still ⬜; Docs/QA/ARCHIVE.md lists exactly ONE pass ever: QA-BUILD-COMPILE, commit 5144ad269, 2026-08-14, Caleb; Docs/QA/RESULTS/ contains a single session file (2026-08-14-caleb.md) plus TEMPLATE.md; QA-PRISM-OCCLUSION is P0 with the failure mode 'an HLSL compile failure turns every prism magenta on load'; QA-PALETTE-SHIELDED records 'Colorimetry verified by simulation only — the engine has never rendered any of it'; Assets/_SO_Assets/Color Palettes/OriginalColorSetSO.asset — the tier pairs the simulation was run against; Assets/_Graphics/Materials/Graphs/{BlockGraph,ExplodingBlockGraph}.shadergraph carry the PrismOcclusion splice; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Vessel/VesselController.cs:163-168, 292-297 bind it under `IsLocalPilot`)

### Verdict
This is an unusually reliable audit on the claims most likely to be wrong. The CI story — the part that could only be checked against run history and that the project's own documentation denies — checks out precisely: a self-hosted Windows runner (`cosmic-build-win`) really does compile and run the edit-mode suite on every trunk push (183 runs, run 183 green 2026-09-03 in 5m14s, and bleeding-edge-guard.yml:224-262 confirms the step genuinely invokes `-runTests`, so ~1,341 tests were green on trunk that day), while Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §0 still says "Authored, not running". Nearly every file-and-line citation I opened was accurate; the party-slot correction (6, not 4) and the DX12-only pin are real. Three corrections matter. **First, the scheduled player-build tier is weaker than reported**: the green 26-minute IL2CPP build of 2026-09-01 built `development` (unity-ci.yml:123-125 sets `REF="development"` for the Tuesday cron), trunk's last green player link was 2026-08-27, the 2026-09-03 Thursday build was *cancelled*, and in every scheduled run the "Edit-mode tests" step is `skipped` — so the branch that gets tagged for the Friday internal build has been unproven for ten days. **Second, multiplayer should be partial, not live**: `MultiplayerSetup.cs:453-461` approves every connection unconditionally and `ProtocolVersion: 8` is a hand-set constant unrelated to the build, so two EA patch levels will connect and desync rather than be refused — on top of the open 🔴 B5 second-joiner bug at exactly the party size the product is sold on. **Third, the audit misses three defects a player hits directly and one that decides the competitive pitch**: `ArcadeGameCoOpWildlifeBlitz` points at a scene absent from `EditorBuildSettings.asset`, so that mode is a guaranteed load failure in the shipped build; `GameplayShaderWarmup.shadervariants` is wired into bootstrap but contains `m_Shaders: {}`, so the pre-warm compiles nothing and first-play hitching is unmitigated; the entire accessibility tab (colourblind, subtitles, reduce-flashing, camera shake) stores values that have zero subscribers, by its own admission; and scoring is client-asserted end to end — `ReportFaunaKill_ServerRpc()` takes no arguments and just increments, `ReportEnvironmentPrismDestroyed_ServerRpc` trusts any non-negative volume, and the weekly leaderboard time is submitted straight from the client, which makes the one competitive surface in the product forgeable in a paid game. Finally, the build-identity gap the auditor correctly rates critical is wider than described: `Application.version` also stamps every analytics event and `PlayerDataService.LastAppVersion`, so PostHog cannot segment by build and no update-detection branch can ever fire.
- CORRECTION Scheduled IL2CPP player-build tier: partial -> partial — but weaker than stated: trunk has had no green player build since 2026-08-27, and the tier does not run tests: The auditor's diagnosis of WHY 2026-09-01 was red (branch drift — `development` lacks `Tools/Shaders/wire_prism_backface_fade.py`) is exactly right and I confirm it. What is wrong is the conclusion drawn: the branch that gets tagged for the Friday internal build is bleeding-edge, and its last proven IL2CPP link is ten days before the audit date, with the most recent attempt cancelled mid-run. (unity-ci.yml:123-125 — the Tuesday cron sets `MODE=il2cpp; REF="development"`. The green 26-minute build the auditor cites (run 33535567840, job 99948949609, 'Player build' 17:04:31→17:30:28) therefore built `development`, NOT bleeding-edge. GitHub reports head_branch=bleeding-edge only because scheduled runs report the default branch; unity-ci.yml:146-148 says so explicitly.; Last green IL2CPP of bleeding-edge itself: run 33108459425, 2026-08-27 (27 min). The Thursday bleeding-edge build after it — run 33760167895, job 100664471793 — has 'Player build' conclusion `cancelled`.; In BOTH scheduled runs, job step 5 'Edit-mode tests' has conclusion `skipped`. The scheduled tier links a player; it does not run the 1,341 tests. Only bleeding-edge-guard.yml does.; unity-ci.yml step 'Verify build output' + 'Upload build manifest' are the only post-build steps — no artefact, no launch.)
- CORRECTION Netcode + Relay multiplayer, parties, presence lobby: live -> partial: The status should follow the auditor's own notes. A stack whose join path has an open red bug at the exact party size the product is sold on ('the party game for pilots', 2–4 players) and which cannot refuse a mismatched build is not 'live' for a paid Steam release — it is partial. Two-player parties working is real, and I confirm the packages, tick rate and eager-Relay design. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:453-461 — `OnConnectionApprovalCallback` sets `response.Approved = true` unconditionally. No build-version check, no capacity check, no identity check, no payload read at all.; Assets/_Prefabs/CORE/NetworkManager.prefab:52 `ProtocolVersion: 8` — a hand-set constant with no relationship to `bundleVersion` (0.2.0) or to any CI value. Two different EA patch levels will connect to each other and desync on the first changed NetworkVariable struct rather than being refused. `ForceSamePrefabs: 1` (line 71) catches only prefab-list differences.; Docs/PartySystem/BUGS.md:16 and :356 — B5 'TC2/TC4 second joiner fails to join' is still 🔴, and :1085 lists the fix order as B2 → B5 → B7. The auditor's own notes concede 3–4-player joining is unproven.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:479-491 — host loss bounces the client to a solo menu (`PartyInviteController.HandleHostLossAsync`). There is graceful recovery but no migration and no rejoin, so a party genuinely dies with its host.)
- CORRECTION Steam Deck / Linux target: partial -> planned: 'Partial' implies a working-but-unverified build path. The code truth is that the scripted, CI-driven pipeline — which Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §2 explicitly names as the single source of truth over Build Profile assets — cannot target Linux at all. A Linux build today requires hand-driving the editor against a profile nobody has exercised. Everything else in the auditor's entry (DX12-only pin, zero Deck/Proton references, no rebinding) I confirm. (Assets/_Scripts/Editor/Build/CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.cs:103-104 hardcodes `target = BuildTarget.StandaloneWindows64, targetGroup = BuildTargetGroup.Standalone`. There is no Linux branch, no target parameter, and `ConfigurePlayerSettings` only ever touches `NamedBuildTarget.Standalone`.; Tools/Build/build_windows.sh is the only build script in the tree; no Linux equivalent.; No CI tier builds Linux (unity-ci.yml modes are compile/mono/il2cpp, all StandaloneWindows64 via the pipeline).; The only Linux artefact is `Assets/Settings/Build Profiles/CS Linux build profile.asset` plus the `com.unity.toolchain.win-x86_64-linux-x86_64` package and FMOD `.so`s — i.e. the ingredients, with nothing that assembles them.)
- CORRECTION Build identity (version / commit stamping): absent -> absent — with a wider blast radius than stated: Status is right; the consequence is larger. With `LastAppVersion` never changing, any 'has this player updated?' branch is permanently false, and the analytics stack the team is relying on for balance-by-data cannot tell one EA patch from another. This strengthens the auditor's 'critical' rating rather than softening it. (Confirmed: ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset:150 `bundleVersion: 0.2.0`; :178-180 `buildNumber: Standalone: 0`; CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.cs:191-197 writes `commit=` only into `build_manifest.txt`, which does not ship inside the player's own reporting.; MISSED consumer: Assets/_Scripts/System/Instrumentation/AnalyticsServiceFacade.cs:416 stamps `["app_version"] = Application.version` on EVERY analytics event. So PostHog and UGS Analytics cannot segment retention, funnels or crash-adjacent metrics by build either — not just Cloud Diagnostics.; Other consumers confirmed: CrashReportingService.cs:85 (`build_version`), GameSettingsPanelController.cs:210 (player-visible), UI/VersionDisplay.cs:14, PlayerDataService.cs:134 (`LastAppVersion`, which also gates first-run/update logic against a string that never changes).)
- ADDITION [dead] Build-settings scene coverage — one shipped game mode's scene is not in the build: Launching this card from the arcade in a player build calls `SceneManager.LoadScene` on a scene the player does not contain — a hard failure, not a degraded mode. The doc and the SO both say it ships; the build settings say it cannot. No CI tier catches this because 'Verify build output' only checks that an .exe exists, and no QA session has ever launched the built player (Docs/QA/QA_BACKLOG.md QA-BUILD-WINDOWS-PLAYER ⬜). This is exactly the class of defect the auditor's 'CI never boots the player' gap predicts, and it is already present. (ProjectSettings/EditorBuildSettings.asset lists 23 scenes; `Assets/_Scenes/Multiplayer Scenes/MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp.unity` is NOT among them, although the file exists on disk (`find Assets/_Scenes -name '*.unity'`).; Assets/_SO_Assets/Games/ArcadeGameCoOpWildlifeBlitz.asset:24 `SceneName: MinigameWildlifeBlitzMultuplayerCoOp`, Mode: 32, IsMultiplayer: 1, Min/MaxPlayersAllowed: 2 — a real, populated arcade card.; Assets/_Scripts/Editor/Build/CosmicShoreBuildPipeline.cs:186 `EnabledScenes()` = `EditorBuildSettings.scenes.Where(s => s.enabled)`, so the shipping player contains exactly that list and nothing else.; CLAUDE.md documents `CoOpWildlifeBlitz(32)` / `CoOpWildlifeBlitzMiniGame` as a live multiplayer mode.)
- ADDITION [dead] Shader pre-warm (first-play compilation hitching): The mechanism, the wiring, the timing (behind the splash) and the logging are all correct and shipped; the collection was never populated, so nothing is warmed. On a DX12-only Windows build with a prism shader family carrying screen-door occlusion, clock animation, shield morphs and per-element crystal graphs, every variant compiles on first use — i.e. the first seconds of the first arena, which on the heaviest cells (Atlantis ~69k prisms, Lattice ~43k grown) is also the worst moment for it. Nothing in CI or QA would detect this; it presents as 'the game stutters when you start playing'. (Assets/_SO_Assets/GameplayShaderWarmup.shadervariants — the whole asset is 10 lines and its payload is `m_Shaders: {}`. Zero variants.; Assets/_SO_Assets/BootstrapConfig.asset:20-21 wires that empty collection into `_shaderWarmupCollections`.; Assets/_Scripts/System/AppManager.cs:283-303 `WarmUpShaders()` iterates the collections, calls `WarmUp()` and logs `variantCount` — which will read 0 every launch.; Assets/_Scripts/System/Bootstrap/BootstrapConfigSO.cs:32,43 declare the field and accessor.)
- ADDITION [absent] Anti-cheat / server validation of client-reported scoring and leaderboard submission: Ownership (`RequireOwnership = true`) prevents crediting ANOTHER player, and the code comments correctly reason about that — but it does not prevent crediting YOURSELF arbitrarily. Four of the sixteen modes and the entire weekly leaderboard are decided by numbers a client asserts. There is also no rate limiting on any of these RPCs. The auditor covered netcode plumbing and cost but not integrity; for a paid game whose pitch includes a weekly competitive board, this is the difference between a leaderboard and a suggestion box. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Player/Player.cs:161-169 — `ReportFaunaKill_ServerRpc()` takes NO parameters and simply does `RoundStats.LifeformsKilled++`. The server never verifies a creature died. This is the sole scoring path for Wildlife Liberation (`ScoringMetric.LifeformsKilled`).; Player.cs:237-252 — `ReportEnvironmentPrismDestroyed_ServerRpc(float volume, int prismDomain)` accepts any client-supplied `volume` with the single guard `if (volume < 0f) return;`. This is the client-side scoring path for Rampage and Peel the Cage, both of which race to 2000 destroyed prisms.; Player.cs:190-208 — `ReportCombatHit_ServerRpc(int hitClass)` validates only that the enum member exists (`Enum.IsDefined`), never that a hit occurred. Sole client scoring path for Dog Fight and The Bends.; Assets/_Scripts/System/WeeklyChallenge/WeeklyChallengeLeaderboardService.cs:160-190 — the completion time is submitted from the CLIENT via `LeaderboardsService.Instance.AddPlayerScoreAsync(id, seconds, options)` with only a `seconds < 0 || NaN || Infinity` sanity check. A modified client submits 0.01 s and holds rank 1 for the week.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:453-461 — connection approval accepts every peer unconditionally, so there is no integrity check at the door either.)
- ADDITION [partial] Accessibility settings (colourblind, subtitles, photosensitivity, camera shake): The settings panel presents five accessibility controls that persist a value and change nothing on screen. This is a UI that lies to the player, and 'reduce flashing' is the one where that is not merely disappointing — a photosensitive player toggles it, sees it stick, and gets the same prism-explosion flashing. It also blocks the Steam store accessibility fields, which are a store-page item alongside the privacy policy the auditor did flag. (Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Settings/AccessibilitySettings.cs:14-56 — a PlayerPrefs-backed static store with five settings and five change events (`OnColorblindModeChanged`, `OnSubtitlesChanged`, `OnSubtitleScaleChanged`, `OnReduceFlashingChanged`, `OnCameraShakeChanged`).; The ONLY references anywhere outside that file are `GameSettingsPanelController.cs:360-362` (setters) and :433-435 (getters). Zero subscribers to any of the five events across `Assets/_Scripts`.; The class's own doc comment (lines 7-12) says so: '...so the eventual consumers — a colorblind screen-space correction, the subtitle renderer (Dialogue System), the flash-limiter, and the camera shake driver — can subscribe and react. The values persist today; wiring each consumer is a follow-up feature.')
- ADDITION [partial] Settings roaming — graphics and accessibility are machine-local: The auditor credited Cloud Save with roaming 'settings'; only audio/invert/joystick roam. On Windows, PlayerPrefs is the registry (HKCU\Software\Froglet Games\Cosmic Shore), so every graphics and accessibility choice is lost on a new machine, a reinstall that clears the key, or a different Windows account — compounding the device-bound-identity gap the auditor correctly rated major. No Steam Cloud exists to cover it (no Steamworks SDK). (Assets/_Scripts/System/CloudData/Models/PlayerSettingsCloudData.cs:28-39 — the cloud settings record carries ONLY `MusicEnabled/SFXEnabled/HapticsEnabled/InvertYEnabled/InvertThrottleEnabled/JoystickVisualsEnabled` plus three levels and a timestamp.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Settings/DisplayGraphicsSettings.cs:78-92 writes display mode, resolution, vsync, frame cap, FOV, quality, AA, render scale, upscaling, texture quality and adaptive-performance to `PlayerPrefs` under the prefix `P` — never to a repository.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Settings/AccessibilitySettings.cs:16,26-54 — same, prefix `a11y.`.; Assets/_Scripts/System/CloudData/Repositories/ has a `PlayerSettingsRepository.cs` but no graphics or accessibility repository.)
- ADDITION [absent] Netcode build-compatibility gate (patch-day cross-version play): Distinct from anti-cheat, and it is the classic Early Access failure: the day a patch ships, a player who has not restarted Steam joins a patched host, connects successfully, and desyncs or drops. `ForceSamePrefabs: 1` catches a changed prefab LIST but not changed RPC signatures, NetworkVariable layouts or scoring rules — all of which this project changes weekly. The cheap fix (write `Application.version` into `ConnectionData` and refuse a mismatch in the approval callback) is ~10 lines and depends on the build-identity work the auditor already ranked critical. (Assets/_Prefabs/CORE/NetworkManager.prefab:52 `ProtocolVersion: 8` — a hand-authored constant, not derived from `bundleVersion` or the CI run.; Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Multiplayer/MultiplayerSetup.cs:453-461 — approval reads no payload and refuses nobody; `NetworkConfig.ConnectionData` in the prefab (line 63) is empty.; MultiplayerSetup.cs:367-386, :426-430 — public sessions are queried and joined by `gameMode` + `maxPlayers` properties only; no version property is written or filtered on.; Nothing in the tree bumps ProtocolVersion (single occurrence, in the prefab).)
- ADDITION [live] FMOD desktop audio ship path: Worth recording because the auditor's inventory covers audio only as documentation drift ('README says Wwise'), and 'does the shipped Windows player have sound' is a platform-readiness question with a non-obvious answer here: the shipped banks live OUTSIDE Assets/ and reach the player through an FMOD editor build callback rather than through anything in `CosmicShoreBuildPipeline` or the CI workflow. It works, but neither the build script nor CI verifies it, so a bank-cache problem on the runner would ship a silent build with a green CI. (Assets/Plugins/FMOD/Resources/FMODStudioSettings.asset:383-384 `sourceProjectPath: Cosmic Shore/Cosmic Shore.fspro`, `sourceBankPath: Cosmic Shore/Build`; :392 `ImportType: 0` (StreamingAssets); :394 `TargetAssetPath: FMODBanks`; :407-410 `MasterBanks: [Master]`, `Banks: [SFX]`.; Desktop banks are tracked in git: `Cosmic Shore/Build/Desktop/Master.bank`, `Master.strings.bank`, `SFX.bank` (git ls-files).; Assets/Plugins/FMOD/src/Editor/EditorSettings.cs:623-661 — `class BuildProcessor : IPreprocessBuildWithReport` calls `EditorSettings.Instance.PreprocessBuild(...)`, which is what copies banks into StreamingAssets at build time; `Assets/StreamingAssets/` itself contains no tracked banks (only the .meta), so the copy is the mechanism.; 12 FMOD platform entries are configured (FMODStudioSettings.asset:424-436).)
- ADDITION [live] Engine version pin (Unity 6000.3.17f1, non-LTS stream): The pin itself is disciplined and CI-enforced, which is a genuine strength the auditor did not list. The risk it carries is unstated anywhere in the docs: a 12-month Early Access on a non-LTS release stream means a forced engine upgrade inside the EA window when 6.3's support window closes, and that upgrade lands on a 1,481-file single assembly with no play-mode tests. Worth naming next to the assembly-split debt, because the two compound. (ProjectSettings/ProjectVersion.txt — `m_EditorVersion: 6000.3.17f1` (Unity 6.3, not the 6000.0 LTS line).; Docs/BUILD_AND_DELIVERY.md §1 pins it: 'Unity 6000.3.17f1 (must match ProjectSettings/ProjectVersion.txt exactly)'.; .github/workflows/unity-ci.yml:19-20 and bleeding-edge-guard.yml:248 both resolve the expected version from ProjectVersion.txt and require the runner's UNITY_PATH to match.)
